# Motorola 68000

The 68000 presents eight 32-bit data registers and eight address-register
names. Byte, word, and long operations share those registers; a 16-bit operation
word selects their size and addressing modes. The active stack pointer is A7,
backed by separate user and supervisor storage. Logical addresses retain 32
bits while the physical address bus exposes 24.

This chapter owns stored state, derived register and status views, and every
documented instruction body and encoding, including software exception requests.
Its formal definitions generate the instructions used by the emulator.
Effective-address decoding and staged register updates are chapter-owned too.
Exception entry, reset, and retirement still use the native core. The [model contract](../../../../docs/cpus/68000/model.md)
describes those boundaries and links the hardware references; the
[language guide](../../../../docs/cpus/literate-specifications.md) explains the notation.

## Stored state

D0–D7 and A0–A6 are independent 32-bit registers. USP and SSP store the two
stack pointers; there is no additional stored A7. PC retains its full logical
address, IR the current operation word, and the interrupt mask three bits.
The entry group records exception-frame bookkeeping, separate from architectural
flags. HALTED, FAULTED, and TRACEPENDING support the execution boundary.

```cpu
cpu "68000"
state {
  register D0: 32
  register D1: 32
  register D2: 32
  register D3: 32
  register D4: 32
  register D5: 32
  register D6: 32
  register D7: 32
  register A0: 32
  register A1: 32
  register A2: 32
  register A3: 32
  register A4: 32
  register A5: 32
  register A6: 32
  register USP: 32
  register SSP: 32
  register PC: 32
  register IR: 16
  register INTERRUPTMASK: 3 = interruptMask
  latch HALTED
  latch FAULTED
  latch TRACEPENDING = tracePending
  group ENTRY {
    choice KIND: "none", "reset", "fault", "exception", "trap"
    register VECTOR: 8
  }
  flag X
  flag N
  flag Z
  flag V
  flag C
  flag T
  flag S
}
```

## Construction and inspection

`new Cpu68000(memory, initialState, connections?)` requires a 16 MiB address
space and all these stored fields:

| Fields | Constraint |
| --- | --- |
| `d0`–`d7` | Unsigned 32-bit data registers |
| `a0`–`a6` | Unsigned 32-bit address registers |
| `usp`, `ssp` | Unsigned 32-bit user and supervisor stack pointers |
| `pc` | Unsigned 32-bit program counter |
| `ir` | Unsigned 16-bit last completely fetched operation word |
| `interruptMask` | Integer from 0 through 7 |
| `halted` | Boolean STOP latch; required in initial state |
| `faulted` | Boolean terminal double-fault latch; only external reset releases it |
| `tracePending` | Boolean owed-trace latch; required independently of T and STOP |
| `entry.kind` | `none`, `reset`, `fault`, `exception`, or `trap`: phase awaiting its first opcode fetch |
| `entry.vector` | Unsigned byte identifying that exception vector; ignored for `none` and `reset` |
| `flags.x/n/z/v/c` | Boolean extend, negative, zero, overflow, and carry |
| `flags.t/s` | Boolean trace and supervisor bits |

The original status register has X/N/Z/V/C at bits 4–0, interrupt mask at
10–8, S at 13, and T at 15. There is no master-mode bit or second trace bit.
Instructions pack and unpack SR/CCR from these fields; snapshots keep no duplicate
packed register. STOP sets `halted`; completed instructions that began with T
set owe a trace through `tracePending`. All three latches are stored in snapshots. `faulted` takes priority over STOP,
trace, and interrupt recognition. `ir` changes as soon as both opcode bytes
have been read; extension fetches, trace, and interrupt entry preserve it.
An odd instruction PC therefore stacks the supplied or previously fetched IR.
Synchronous exceptions and trace ignore the interrupt mask; external requests
use the level rules below. Entry clears T and selects SSP.

The exported `cpu68000StateDescription` is generated from the state block above.
It supplies field names and constraints to construction and machine parsing.
Construction reads each declared field once, validates it, and owns a copy.
Numeric and named-choice errors throw `RangeError`; missing or invalid flag groups and Boolean
fields throw `TypeError`. Extra metadata and derived views are ignored.
Construction does not read RAM, reset, or execute.

`snapshot()` owns a detached copy with readonly TypeScript types. It adds:

- `a7`: `ssp` when S is set, otherwise `usp`. The active pointer is a view,
  so switching modes cannot leave a duplicate A7 value out of sync.
- `physicalPc`: the low 24 bits of `pc`.

Snapshots can initialize another CPU. Neither inspection nor snapshot copying
accesses RAM. Mutating caller state or an earlier snapshot cannot alter the CPU.

`entry` preserves the context of the next initial opcode fetch across snapshots.
Ordinary initial state uses `{ kind: "none", vector: 0 }`. Successful reset
sets `reset`; bus/address-error entry sets `fault`; group-1 exceptions, trace,
and interrupts set `exception`; group-2 instruction traps set `trap`.
The vector distinguishes, for example, TRAP #0 from TRAP #1. A complete opcode
fetch clears the context to `none`, vector 0; a partial fetch preserves it.
This records entry sequencing without storing a prefetch queue. The existing
trace/interrupt boundary priority still applies before that fetch.

## A7 and physical addresses

S selects the stack bank at each operand's turn: clear means USP, set means
SSP. Reading A7 reads only that selected register. Capturing the choice separately
lets an exchange resolve both register identities before reading either value,
and retain those identities through writeback. The bank view returns zero for
user storage and one for supervisor storage.

```cpu
view STACKBANK "active stack bank" : 8 {
  supervisor = flag S
  return select(supervisor, u8(1), u8(0))
}
view A7 "active stack pointer" : 32 {
  bank = source STACKBANK
  pointer = choose not(zero(bank)) : 32 {
    then {
      stack = register SSP
      return stack
    }
    else {
      stack = register USP
      return stack
    }
  }
  return pointer
}
view PHYSICALPC "physical program address" : 32 {
  logical = register PC
  return and(logical, u32($FFFFFF))
}
```

The three-bit address code names A0–A7. Selecting A7 captures its current bank;
internal codes seven and eight then identify USP and SSP respectively. This
selection reads S only for A7 and does not read either stack pointer yet.

```cpu
codes addressRegisters {
  000 "A0"
  001 "A1"
  010 "A2"
  011 "A3"
  100 "A4"
  101 "A5"
  110 "A6"
  111 "A7"
}
source selectAddressRegister "select address-register storage" (code: 3) : 8 {
  selected = choose zero(xor(code, u3(7))) : 8 {
    then {
      bank = source STACKBANK
      return add(u8(7), bank)
    }
    else {
      return extend(code, 8)
    }
  }
  return selected
}
operands addressStorage {
  000 "A0" = register A0
  001 "A1" = register A1
  010 "A2" = register A2
  011 "A3" = register A3
  100 "A4" = register A4
  101 "A5" = register A5
  110 "A6" = register A6
  111 "USP" = register USP
}
source readAddressRegister "read selected address register" (selected: 8) : 32 {
  contents = match selected : 32 {
    case "00000rrr" for r in addressStorage {
      original = operand r
      return original
    }
    case "00001000" {
      original = register SSP
      return original
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
  return contents
}
action writeAddressRegister "write selected address register" (selected: 8, contents: 32) {
  match selected {
    case "00000rrr" for r in addressStorage {
      operand r <- contents
    }
    case "00001000" {
      SSP <- contents
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
}
```

## Condition codes and status

CCR packs X, N, Z, V, C into bits 4 through 0. SR additionally holds T in bit
15, S in bit 13, and the interrupt mask in bits 10–8; reserved bits read as zero.
Capture SR in the established order: T, S, interrupt mask, then X/N/Z/V/C.
CCR writes update those five flags in place. Full SR writes then update T/S
and finally the interrupt mask, preserving the existing flag object.

```cpu
view CCR "condition code register" : 16 {
  x = flag X
  n = flag N
  z = flag Z
  v = flag V
  c = flag C
  return or(or(or(or(or(u16(0), select(x, u16($10), u16(0))), select(n, u16($8), u16(0))), select(z, u16($4), u16(0))), select(v, u16($2), u16(0))), select(c, u16($1), u16(0)))
}
view SR "status register" : 16 {
  trace = flag T
  supervisor = flag S
  mask = register INTERRUPTMASK
  condition = source CCR
  return or(or(or(select(trace, u16($8000), u16(0)), select(supervisor, u16($2000), u16(0))), shiftBits(extend(mask, 16), left, 8)), condition)
}
policy conditionCode "restore condition codes" (contents: 16) {
  X = not(zero(and(contents, u16($10))))
  N = not(zero(and(contents, u16($8))))
  Z = not(zero(and(contents, u16($4))))
  V = not(zero(and(contents, u16($2))))
  C = not(zero(and(contents, u16($1))))
}
policy systemFlags "restore system flags" (contents: 16) {
  T = not(zero(and(contents, u16($8000))))
  S = not(zero(and(contents, u16($2000))))
}
action writeCCR "write condition code register" (contents: 16) {
  apply conditionCode(contents)
}
action writeSR "write status register" (contents: 16) {
  perform writeCCR(contents)
  apply systemFlags(contents)
  INTERRUPTMASK <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 8), 3)
}
```

## Data-register results

A byte or word write preserves the live upper part of its data register.
The three result policies set N from the size's sign bit, set Z for zero, and
clear V/C. They preserve X/T/S and also serve the remaining TypeScript families.

```cpu
operands dataRegisters {
  000 "D0" = register D0
  001 "D1" = register D1
  010 "D2" = register D2
  011 "D3" = register D3
  100 "D4" = register D4
  101 "D5" = register D5
  110 "D6" = register D6
  111 "D7" = register D7
}
policy byteResult "68000 result" (result: 8) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = 0
  C = 0
}
policy wordResult "68000 result" (result: 16) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = 0
  C = 0
}
policy longResult "68000 result" (result: 32) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = 0
  C = 0
}
```

## Register transfers

MOVE uses `00 zz ddd mmm sss rrr`: `zz=01` is byte, `10` long, `11` word.
The destination occupies `ddd/mmm`; the source `sss/rrr`. Mode `000` names Dn,
mode `001` An. Byte forms exclude address registers. Destination mode `001`
selects MOVEA, which sign-extends a word and preserves every flag.

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Write the destination before applying the result flags; preserve its live upper bits for a byte or word.

```cpu
family move8DataData "0001 ddd 000 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.B {r},{d}" {
  originalSource = operand r
  result = truncate(originalSource, 8)
  preserved = operand d
  operand d <- or(and(preserved, u32($FFFFFF00)), extend(result, 32))
  apply byteResult(result)
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Write the destination before applying the result flags; preserve its live upper bits for a byte or word.

```cpu
family registerCopy "0011 ddd 000 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.W {r},{d}" {
  originalSource = operand r
  result = truncate(originalSource, 16)
  preserved = operand d
  operand d <- or(and(preserved, u32($FFFF0000)), extend(result, 32))
  apply wordResult(result)
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Write the destination before applying the result flags; preserve its live upper bits for a byte or word.

```cpu
family move16AddressData "0011 ddd 000 001 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in addressRegisters named "MOVE.W {r},{d}" {
  sourceCode = operand r
  sourceRegister = source selectAddressRegister(sourceCode)
  originalSource = source readAddressRegister(sourceRegister)
  result = truncate(originalSource, 16)
  preserved = operand d
  operand d <- or(and(preserved, u32($FFFF0000)), extend(result, 32))
  apply wordResult(result)
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Replace the complete address register and preserve every flag.

```cpu
family move16DataAddress "0011 ddd 001 000 rrr" for d in addressRegisters, r in dataRegisters named "MOVEA.W {r},{d}" {
  originalSource = operand r
  result = truncate(originalSource, 16)
  destinationCode = operand d
  destinationRegister = source selectAddressRegister(destinationCode)
  perform writeAddressRegister(destinationRegister, signExtend(result, 32))
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Replace the complete address register and preserve every flag.

```cpu
family move16AddressAddress "0011 ddd 001 001 rrr" for d in addressRegisters, r in addressRegisters named "MOVEA.W {r},{d}" {
  sourceCode = operand r
  sourceRegister = source selectAddressRegister(sourceCode)
  originalSource = source readAddressRegister(sourceRegister)
  result = truncate(originalSource, 16)
  destinationCode = operand d
  destinationRegister = source selectAddressRegister(destinationCode)
  perform writeAddressRegister(destinationRegister, signExtend(result, 32))
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Write the destination before applying the result flags; preserve its live upper bits for a byte or word.

```cpu
family move32DataData "0010 ddd 000 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.L {r},{d}" {
  originalSource = operand r
  result = originalSource
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Write the destination before applying the result flags; preserve its live upper bits for a byte or word.

```cpu
family move32AddressData "0010 ddd 000 001 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in addressRegisters named "MOVE.L {r},{d}" {
  sourceCode = operand r
  sourceRegister = source selectAddressRegister(sourceCode)
  originalSource = source readAddressRegister(sourceRegister)
  result = originalSource
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Replace the complete address register and preserve every flag.

```cpu
family move32DataAddress "0010 ddd 001 000 rrr" for d in addressRegisters, r in dataRegisters named "MOVEA.L {r},{d}" {
  originalSource = operand r
  result = originalSource
  destinationCode = operand d
  destinationRegister = source selectAddressRegister(destinationCode)
  perform writeAddressRegister(destinationRegister, result)
}
```

Capture the source before selecting the destination. Replace the complete address register and preserve every flag.

```cpu
family move32AddressAddress "0010 ddd 001 001 rrr" for d in addressRegisters, r in addressRegisters named "MOVEA.L {r},{d}" {
  sourceCode = operand r
  sourceRegister = source selectAddressRegister(sourceCode)
  originalSource = source readAddressRegister(sourceRegister)
  result = originalSource
  destinationCode = operand d
  destinationRegister = source selectAddressRegister(destinationCode)
  perform writeAddressRegister(destinationRegister, result)
}
```

## Small constants, extension, and word exchange

MOVEQ encodes `0111 ddd 0 iiiiiiii`. Its signed eight-bit constant becomes a
complete long in Dn. The native operation-word decoder still passes the low byte
as an input; the chapter owns the encoding inventory and the body.

Sign-extend the immediate byte, replace Dn, then set N/Z and clear V/C.
Preserve X/T/S.

```cpu
family moveQuick (immediate: 8) "0111 ddd 0 xxxxxxxx" for d in dataRegisters named "MOVEQ #n,{d}" {
  result = signExtend(immediate, 32)
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

EXT occupies `0100 1000 1 s 000 rrr`, with `s=0` for byte-to-word and `s=1`
for word-to-long. EXT.W preserves the upper word from the same single read;
there is no live reread of Dn between calculation and writeback.

Read Dn once, sign-extend its byte to a word, preserve the captured upper word,
and write Dn before applying word-result flags.

```cpu
family extendWord "0100 1000 10 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "EXT.W {r}" {
  original = operand r
  result = signExtend(truncate(original, 8), 16)
  operand r <- or(and(original, u32($FFFF0000)), extend(result, 32))
  apply wordResult(result)
}
```

Read Dn once, sign-extend its word to a long, and write Dn before applying
long-result flags.

```cpu
family extendLong "0100 1000 11 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "EXT.L {r}" {
  original = operand r
  result = signExtend(truncate(original, 16), 32)
  operand r <- result
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

SWAP uses `0100 1000 01 000 rrr`, beside the EXT slots. Read Dn once and
exchange its high and low words. Apply long-result flags before writing Dn,
preserving the established execution order.

```cpu
family swap "0100 1000 01 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "SWAP {r}" {
  original = operand r
  result = or(shiftBits(original, left, 16), shiftBits(original, right, 16))
  apply longResult(result)
  operand r <- result
}
```

## Register exchange

EXG uses `1100 ddd 1 ooooo rrr`. Operation `01000` exchanges Dn/Dn,
`01001` An/An, and `10001` Dn/An. Both identities must be selected before
either value is read, especially when A7 participates. Capture both originals,
write left then right, and preserve every flag, even for self-exchanges.

Resolve both register identities before reading either original. Capture both
values before writing left then right; preserve every flag.

```cpu
family exchangeDataData "1100 ddd 1 01000 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in dataRegisters named "EXG {d},{r}" {
  left = operand d
  right = operand r
  operand d <- right
  operand r <- left
}
```

Resolve both register identities before reading either original. Capture both
values before writing left then right; preserve every flag.

```cpu
family exchangeAddressAddress "1100 ddd 1 01001 rrr" for d in addressRegisters, r in addressRegisters named "EXG {d},{r}" {
  leftCode = operand d
  leftRegister = source selectAddressRegister(leftCode)
  rightCode = operand r
  rightRegister = source selectAddressRegister(rightCode)
  left = source readAddressRegister(leftRegister)
  right = source readAddressRegister(rightRegister)
  perform writeAddressRegister(leftRegister, right)
  perform writeAddressRegister(rightRegister, left)
}
```

Resolve both register identities before reading either original. Capture both
values before writing left then right; preserve every flag.

```cpu
family exchangeDataAddress "1100 ddd 1 10001 rrr" for d in dataRegisters, r in addressRegisters named "EXG {d},{r}" {
  rightCode = operand r
  rightRegister = source selectAddressRegister(rightCode)
  left = operand d
  right = source readAddressRegister(rightRegister)
  operand d <- right
  perform writeAddressRegister(rightRegister, left)
}
```

## Effective-address decoding

An effective address is a 32-bit logical address. The memory connection projects
it onto the physical 24-bit bus later. The six-bit `mmm rrr` field selects a
mode and a register (or a submode when `mmm=111`). Instruction families already
exclude illegal combinations before any extension or operand access.

| `mmm` | Operand | Extensions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `000`, `001` | Dn or An directly; handled by instruction families | None |
| `010` | `(An)` | None |
| `011` | `(An)+` | None |
| `100` | `-(An)` | None |
| `101` | `(d16,An)` | Signed word displacement |
| `110` | `(d8,An,Xn)` | Brief index word |
| `111` | Absolute, PC-relative, or immediate | Selected by `rrr` |

For mode `111`, codes `000` and `001` select sign-extended absolute word and
absolute long addresses. Codes `010` and `011` select PC-relative displacement
and indexed forms. Immediate operands (`100`) belong to the instruction's fetch
sequence, not this address source.

### Stored registers and pending updates

Operand decoding can stage a new address-register value before the instruction
commits it. Later operands and index calculations see that pending value; a
normal register read still sees stored state. Unstaged reads consult live state
at their own turn. Each selected A7 bank is retained independently of later S
changes. Byte auto-updates use two bytes for A7 and one for every other An;
word and long updates use two and four bytes.

`pending register` and `stage` operate on instruction-local bookkeeping, not
extra CPU state. Commit writes the latest value of each selected register in
first-stage order. Repeated commits retain that order and those pending values.
An alignment or extension-fetch failure before commit leaves stored registers
unchanged; failures after commit retain the completed writes. The instruction
families choose those commit points explicitly with `commit addresses`.

The ordinary data reader captures the whole Dn. The address reader below first
checks pending storage and reads the live register only when it has no staged
value.

```cpu
source readDataRegister "read selected data register" (code: 3) : 32 {
  contents = match extend(code, 8) : 32 {
    case "00000rrr" for r in dataRegisters {
      original = operand r
      return original
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
  return contents
}
```

```cpu
source readPendingAddressRegister "read pending or stored address register" (selected: 8) : 32 {
  contents = match selected : 32 {
    case "00000rrr" for r in addressStorage {
      original = pending operand r
      return original
    }
    case "00001000" {
      original = pending register SSP
      return original
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
  return contents
}
action stageAddressRegister "stage selected address register" (selected: 8, contents: 32) using staging {
  match selected {
    case "00000rrr" for r in addressStorage {
      stage operand r <- contents
    }
    case "00001000" {
      stage SSP <- contents
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
}
```

### Brief index extensions

The extension word is `t rrr w 000 dddddddd`: `t=0` selects Dn, `t=1` selects
An, and `w=0/1` selects signed word/full long index values. The low byte is a
signed displacement. This original-68000 model ignores bits 10–8; it implements
neither scaling nor the later full extension format. Decode after fetching the
whole word, so register reads observe completed fetch callbacks. Address-index
reads also observe pending updates. Capture the address-bank selection before
choosing Dn versus An, retaining the existing model's read order.

```cpu
source indexOffset "brief index and signed displacement" (extension: 16) : 32 {
  code = truncate(shiftBits(extension, right, 12), 3)
  bank = source selectAddressRegister(code)
  index = choose not(zero(and(extension, u16($8000)))) : 32 {
    then {
      contents = source readPendingAddressRegister(bank)
      return contents
    }
    else {
      contents = source readDataRegister(code)
      return contents
    }
  }
  wide = select(not(zero(and(extension, u16($0800)))), index, signExtend(truncate(index, 16), 32))
  return add(wide, signExtend(lowByte(extension), 32))
}
```

### Resolving a memory operand

Capture the selected address bank and its pending/live base before fetching any
extensions, including for the absolute and PC-relative submodes. Displacement
and index additions wrap at 32 bits. A PC-relative base is the extension word's
address, captured before that word is fetched. Predecrement returns the staged
new address; postincrement returns the captured old address.

The size input is the operand's width in bits: 8, 16, or 32, supplied by the
checked `resolve` instruction statement. This source returns an address and
stages updates; alignment checks and commit timing remain in the instruction.
Unsupported mode selections return `unsupported` without inventing an address.

```cpu
source effectiveAddress "resolve a logical memory address" (size: 8, mode: 3, code: 3) : 32 {
  bank = source selectAddressRegister(code)
  base = source readPendingAddressRegister(bank)
  bytes = shiftBits(size, right, 3)
  step = select(and(zero(xor(size, u8(8))), zero(xor(code, u3(7)))), u32(2), extend(bytes, 32))
  address = match extend(mode, 8) : 32 {
    case "00000010" {
      return base
    }
    case "00000011" {
      perform stageAddressRegister(bank, add(base, step))
      return base
    }
    case "00000100" {
      decremented = subtract(base, step)
      perform stageAddressRegister(bank, decremented)
      return decremented
    }
    case "00000101" {
      displacement = fetch word
      return add(base, signExtend(displacement, 32))
    }
    case "00000110" {
      extension = fetch word
      offset = source indexOffset(extension)
      return add(base, offset)
    }
    case "00000111" {
      absoluteOrRelative = match extend(code, 8) : 32 {
        case "00000000" {
          absolute = fetch word
          return signExtend(absolute, 32)
        }
        case "00000001" {
          high = fetch word
          low = fetch word
          return concat(high, low)
        }
        case "00000010" {
          basePc = next address
          displacement = fetch word
          return add(basePc, signExtend(displacement, 32))
        }
        case "00000011" {
          basePc = next address
          extension = fetch word
          offset = source indexOffset(extension)
          return add(basePc, offset)
        }
        otherwise unsupported
      }
      return absoluteOrRelative
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
  return address
}
```

## Memory transfers and immediate operands

MOVE uses the same `00 zz ddd mmm sss rrr` layout for every addressing form.
The source is `sss/rrr`; the destination fields occur in the opposite order,
`ddd/mmm`. The following table describes the effective-address fields:

| Mode | Register/code | Operand |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `000` | `000`–`111` | D0–D7 |
| `001` | `000`–`111` | A0–A7; word/long only |
| `010` | `000`–`111` | `(An)` |
| `011` | `000`–`111` | `(An)+` |
| `100` | `000`–`111` | `-(An)` |
| `101` | `000`–`111` | `(d16,An)` |
| `110` | `000`–`111` | `(d8,An,Xn)` |
| `111` | `000`, `001` | Absolute short, absolute long |
| `111` | `010`, `011` | PC-relative displacement, indexed PC-relative; source only |
| `111` | `100` | Immediate; source only |
| `111` | `101`–`111` | Undefined |

Byte transfers exclude An on both sides. MOVEA is selected by destination mode
`001`; it replaces the whole address register, sign-extends a word, and preserves
all flags. PC-relative source reads use program space; other memory operands use
data space. The effective-address resolver still owns extension decoding,
index calculation, A7 byte strides, and staged predecrement/postincrement.

### Ordered byte access

The resolver returns a full 32-bit logical address. Each memory callback applies
the 24-bit physical projection, retaining the logical address and access space
for fault reporting. Words and longs require even addresses. Alignment checks
remain at the instruction level, before the first rejected access; these sources
and actions express only the ordered transfers. Address addition wraps at 32 bits.
A long reads or writes its most significant byte first, through its least
significant byte. A failed access retains earlier bytes and prevents later ones.

```cpu
source readMemoryByte "read 8-bit data memory" (address: 32) : 8 {
  byte0 = memory(address)
  return byte0
}
```

```cpu
source readProgramByte "read 8-bit program memory" (address: 32) : 8 {
  byte0 = program memory(address)
  return byte0
}
```

```cpu
action writeMemoryByte "write 8-bit data memory" (address: 32, contents: 8) using memory {
  memory(address) <- contents
}
```

```cpu
source readMemoryWord "read 16-bit data memory" (address: 32) : 16 {
  byte0 = memory(address)
  byte1 = memory(add(address, u32(1)))
  return concat(byte0, byte1)
}
```

```cpu
source readProgramWord "read 16-bit program memory" (address: 32) : 16 {
  byte0 = program memory(address)
  byte1 = program memory(add(address, u32(1)))
  return concat(byte0, byte1)
}
```

```cpu
action writeMemoryWord "write 16-bit data memory" (address: 32, contents: 16) using memory {
  memory(address) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 8), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(1))) <- truncate(contents, 8)
}
```

```cpu
source readMemoryLong "read 32-bit data memory" (address: 32) : 32 {
  byte0 = memory(address)
  byte1 = memory(add(address, u32(1)))
  byte2 = memory(add(address, u32(2)))
  byte3 = memory(add(address, u32(3)))
  return concat(concat(byte0, byte1), concat(byte2, byte3))
}
```

```cpu
source readProgramLong "read 32-bit program memory" (address: 32) : 32 {
  byte0 = program memory(address)
  byte1 = program memory(add(address, u32(1)))
  byte2 = program memory(add(address, u32(2)))
  byte3 = program memory(add(address, u32(3)))
  return concat(concat(byte0, byte1), concat(byte2, byte3))
}
```

```cpu
action writeMemoryLong "write 32-bit data memory" (address: 32, contents: 32) using memory {
  memory(address) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 24), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(1))) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 16), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(2))) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 8), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(3))) <- truncate(contents, 8)
}
```

Immediate byte operands occupy the low byte of one extension word. A word
fetch supplies all sixteen bits; a long fetches the high word before the low
word. The fetch boundary owns cursor advancement and partial-fetch reporting.

```cpu
source immediateByte "8-bit immediate operand" : 8 {
  high = fetch word
  return truncate(high, 8)
}
```

```cpu
source immediateWord "16-bit immediate operand" : 16 {
  high = fetch word
  return high
}
```

```cpu
source immediateLong "32-bit immediate operand" : 32 {
  high = fetch word
  low = fetch word
  return concat(high, low)
}
```

### Data-register writeback

A byte or word write preserves the destination's **live** upper bits. Read those
bits at writeback, after any source reads, destination extension fetches, and
pending address-register commits. Long writes replace all thirty-two bits.
The three-bit data code selects D0–D7; selection itself performs no state access.

```cpu
codes dataRegisterCodes {
  000 "D0"
  001 "D1"
  010 "D2"
  011 "D3"
  100 "D4"
  101 "D5"
  110 "D6"
  111 "D7"
}
```

```cpu
action writeDataByte "write 8-bit data register" (code: 3, contents: 8) {
  match extend(code, 8) {
    case "00000rrr" for r in dataRegisters {
      preserved = operand r
      operand r <- or(and(preserved, u32($FFFFFF00)), extend(contents, 32))
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
}
```

```cpu
action writeDataWord "write 16-bit data register" (code: 3, contents: 16) {
  match extend(code, 8) {
    case "00000rrr" for r in dataRegisters {
      preserved = operand r
      operand r <- or(and(preserved, u32($FFFF0000)), extend(contents, 32))
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
}
```

```cpu
action writeDataLong "write 32-bit data register" (code: 3, contents: 32) {
  match extend(code, 8) {
    case "00000rrr" for r in dataRegisters {
      operand r <- contents
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
}
```

### MOVE and MOVEA families

The patterns below enumerate every remaining legal pair. `x` positions still
belong to the operation word, but do not select a different instruction body.
The native binding extracts `sourceMode/sourceCode` from `sss/rrr` and
`destinationMode/destinationCode` from `mmm/ddd`. Thus modes with the same
register, memory, and access-space roles share their formal body. The preceding
register families already own the forms where both operands are registers.

Resolve and read the entire source **before** resolving the destination or
fetching its extensions. Later address calculations observe pending updates.
Select a destination A7 bank before committing those updates, and retain that
selection through writeback. Check alignment before committing: a failed source
or destination extension discards pending updates. Commit before a destination
write; a failed write retains those updates and earlier bytes, with flags unchanged.
Only a complete MOVE sets N/Z and clears V/C; MOVEA preserves every flag.

Capture the source byte from Dn before resolving the destination. Commit pending
address updates before writeback. Only completed writeback sets N/Z and clears V/C;
preserve X/T/S. Failed writes retain committed updates and earlier bytes with flags
unchanged.

```cpu
family operandMove8DataMemory (sourceMode: 3, sourceCode: 3, destinationMode: 3, destinationCode: 3) {
  encoding "00 01 xxx 01x 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.B {r},MEMORY"
  encoding "00 01 xxx 10x 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.B {r},MEMORY"
  encoding "00 01 xxx 110 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.B {r},MEMORY"
  encoding "00 01 00x 111 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "MOVE.B {r},MEMORY"
  originalSource = operand r
  result = truncate(originalSource, 8)
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, destinationMode, destinationCode)
  commit addresses
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
  apply byteResult(result)
}
```

Resolve and read the complete byte in data space before selecting the destination.
Commit pending address updates before writeback. Preserve the destination's live
upper 24 bits. Only completed writeback sets N/Z and clears V/C; preserve X/T/S.

```cpu
family operandMove8MemoryData (sourceMode: 3, sourceCode: 3, destinationMode: 3, destinationCode: 3) {
  encoding "00 01 ddd 000 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "00 01 ddd 000 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "00 01 ddd 000 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "00 01 ddd 000 111 00x" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.B MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(8, sourceMode, sourceCode)
  result = source readMemoryByte(sourceAddress)
  registerCode = operand d
  commit addresses
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
  apply byteResult(result)
}
```

Capture the source word from Dn before resolving the destination. Reject odd memory

```cpu
family operandMove16DataMemory (sourceMode: 3, sourceCode: 3, destinationMode: 3, destinationCode: 3) {

  fault alignment write(destinationAddress) if lowBit(destinationAddress)
  commit addresses
  perform writeMemoryWord(destinationAddress, result)
  apply wordResult(result)
}
```

```cpu
family operandMove32MemoryData (sourceMode: 3, sourceCode: 3, destinationMode: 3, destinationCode: 3) {

  encoding "00 10 ddd 000 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.L MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "00 10 ddd 000 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.L MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "00 10 ddd 000 111 00x" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE.L MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(32, sourceMode, sourceCode)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  result = source readMemoryLong(sourceAddress)
  registerCode = operand d
  commit addresses
  perform writeDataLong(registerCode, result)
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

Reject odd memory addresses before their first byte access. Select the destination A7
bank before any pending-update commit. Commit pending address updates before
writeback. Replace the complete address register; preserve every flag.

```cpu
  result = source readMemoryLong(sourceAddress)
  registerCode = operand d
  destinationRegister = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  commit addresses
  perform writeAddressRegister(destinationRegister, result)
}
```

bytes with flags unchanged.

## Logical operations and read/modify/write

AND keeps bits set in both operands, OR keeps bits set in either, and EOR keeps
bits set in exactly one. NOT complements each bit; CLR produces zero; TST sets
condition codes from the original operand without writing a result. All use the
byte/word/long result policies above: N/Z describe the result, V/C clear, and
X/T/S are preserved. Address registers are not legal operands for these families.

### Encodings and operand order

The binary layout is `oooo rrr d ss mmm eee`. `oooo=1000` selects OR,
`1100` AND, and `1011` EOR with `d=1`. `ss=00/01/10` selects byte/word/long;
`ss=11` belongs to other instructions. `rrr` selects a data register, while
`mmm/eee` selects an effective address using the preceding table.

| Direction | Source | Destination | Legal effective addresses |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AND/OR, `d=0` | EA | Dn | Data register, data memory, PC-relative, immediate |
| AND/OR, `d=1` | Dn | EA | Alterable data memory |
| EOR, `d=1` | Dn | EA | Data register or alterable data memory |

Immediate logic uses `0000 ooo 0 ss mmm eee`: `ooo=000/001/101` selects
ORI/ANDI/EORI. Its destination must be a data register or alterable data memory.
AND/OR with an immediate source EA has the same operand effects as ANDI/ORI to
Dn; those encodings select the same formal body below. CCR/SR immediate forms
are separate system instructions and remain outside these families.

Unary logic uses `0100 oooo ss mmm eee`: `oooo=0010/0110/1010` selects
CLR/NOT/TST. Even TST is restricted to data-alterable operands on the original
68000: PC-relative and immediate operands are excluded.

Every family below takes the encoded `mmm/eee` as `mode/code`. Other register
selectors and the chosen calculation are bound by the chapter. Pattern `x`
positions share a body while still determining those runtime EA fields.
No second TypeScript catalogue determines which operation words are legal.

MOVE commits pending address updates after completing the source read and before
writing the destination. Logical operations instead commit **before reading the
destination**, and set flags **before writing it**. The sequence is:

1. Read the complete source, when present, before resolving the destination.
2. Resolve the destination once. Reject odd word/long addresses before any
   access to them or commitment of pending address updates.
3. Commit pending address updates, then read the destination. CLR retains this
   read even though its result ignores the value.
4. Calculate the result and apply N/Z/V/C. TST stops here.
5. Write the result using the captured address or register code. byte/word Dn
   writes preserve the live upper bits, read after the flag updates.

Failed source accesses discard pending updates. Failed destination reads retain
committed updates but leave result flags unchanged. Failed writes retain updated
flags, committed address registers, and completed bytes. All memory transfers
remain high byte first with 32-bit logical wrap; PC-relative source reads retain
program-space identity in both bus and alignment faults.

### Captured registers and pure calculations

The data-register reader selects only D0–D7 and captures the whole register at
its own turn. Narrowing happens in the instruction before calculation. Its
matching write actions above preserve live upper bits at writeback, so a memory
or flag callback cannot make a captured upper part overwrite a later change.



The calculation sources receive already captured numbers; they perform no state
or memory access. An encoding's `with calculation = ...` chooses the expression
while the surrounding family keeps the same visible access and writeback order.
CLR's calculation receives the captured operand too, even though it returns zero.

```cpu
source andByte "8-bit AND result" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  return and(left, right)
}
source orByte "8-bit OR result" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  return or(left, right)
}
source eorByte "8-bit EOR result" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  return xor(left, right)
}
source clearByte "8-bit clear result" (original: 8) : 8 {
  return u8(0)
}
source complementByte "8-bit complement result" (original: 8) : 8 {
  return xor(original, u8($FF))
}
```

```cpu
source andWord "16-bit AND result" (left: 16, right: 16) : 16 {
  return and(left, right)
}
source orWord "16-bit OR result" (left: 16, right: 16) : 16 {
  return or(left, right)
}
source eorWord "16-bit EOR result" (left: 16, right: 16) : 16 {
  return xor(left, right)
}
source clearWord "16-bit clear result" (original: 16) : 16 {
  return u16(0)
}
source complementWord "16-bit complement result" (original: 16) : 16 {
  return xor(original, u16($FFFF))
}
```

```cpu
source andLong "32-bit AND result" (left: 32, right: 32) : 32 {
  return and(left, right)
}
source orLong "32-bit OR result" (left: 32, right: 32) : 32 {
  return or(left, right)
}
source eorLong "32-bit EOR result" (left: 32, right: 32) : 32 {
  return xor(left, right)
}
source clearLong "32-bit clear result" (original: 32) : 32 {
  return u32(0)
}
source complementLong "32-bit complement result" (original: 32) : 32 {
  return xor(original, u32($FFFFFFFF))
}
```

### Binary families

Capture the source byte before reading the destination, including when both select
the same Dn. Calculate the result from those captures and apply N/Z/V/C before
writeback; preserve X/T/S. Read the destination's live upper bits again at writeback.

```cpu
family operandLogic8DataData (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "1000 ddd 0 00 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = orByte named "OR.B {r},{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 0 00 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = andByte named "AND.B {r},{d}"
  encoding "1011 rrr 1 00 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = eorByte named "EOR.B {r},{d}"
  originalSource = operand r
  sourceValue = truncate(originalSource, 8)
  registerCode = operand d
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  apply byteResult(result)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Read the complete byte in data space before committing pending address updates and
reading Dn. A failed source discards pending updates. Calculate and set N/Z/V/C
before writing Dn; preserve X/T/S. Preserve its live upper bits after the flag
updates.

```cpu
family operandLogic8MemoryData (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "1000 ddd 0 00 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = orByte named "OR.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1000 ddd 0 00 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = orByte named "OR.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1000 ddd 0 00 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = orByte named "OR.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1000 ddd 0 00 111 00x" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = orByte named "OR.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 0 00 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = andByte named "AND.B MEMORY,{d}"

  encoding "1100 ddd 0 00 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = andByte named "AND.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 0 00 111 00x" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = andByte named "AND.B MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  sourceValue = source readMemoryByte(sourceAddress)
  commit addresses
  registerCode = operand d
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  apply byteResult(result)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Read the complete byte in program space before committing pending address updates and

```cpu
  encoding "1100 ddd 0 01 111 100" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = andWord named "ANDI.W IMMEDIATE,{d}"
  encoding "0000 1010 01 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = eorWord named "EORI.W IMMEDIATE,{d}"
  sourceValue = source immediateWord
  registerCode = operand d
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 16)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  apply wordResult(result)
  perform writeDataWord(registerCode, result)
}
```

Capture the data-register word before resolving the destination once. Reject an odd
destination before committing updates or reading it. Commit pending updates before
reading the old destination. Set N/Z/V/C before writing the result, preserving X/T/S.
Failed reads retain committed updates; failed writes retain flags and completed
bytes.

### Unary families

Capture the original byte in Dn. CLR retains the original read even though its result
is zero; NOT complements every bit. Set N/Z/V/C before writeback and preserve X/T/S.
Byte/word writes read and preserve live upper bits after the flag updates.

```cpu
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination)
  apply byteResult(result)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
```

## Addition, subtraction, and comparison

The arithmetic unit works at byte, word, or long width. ADD and SUB replace
the destination; CMP performs the same subtraction solely to set N/Z/V/C.
Data arithmetic copies carry or borrow into X, while comparisons preserve X.
ADDX, SUBX, and NEGX consume X and accumulate Z across successive pieces of a
multi-precision number. Address arithmetic has a separate rule: ADDA/SUBA and
ADDQ/SUBQ to An operate on all 32 bits without changing any flag. CMPA compares
all 32 bits and sets N/Z/V/C, preserving X.

### Arithmetic encodings

The main layout is `oooo rrr d ss mmm eee`. `oooo=1001/1011/1101` selects
SUB/CMP/ADD, and `ss=00/01/10` selects byte/word/long. With `d=0`, the EA is
the source and Dn is the destination. An direct sources are legal for word and
long operations only. With `d=1`, ADD/SUB use Dn as source and alterable memory
as destination. CMP's `d=1` encodings instead contain EOR and CMPM.

| Pattern | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `oooo rrr s11 mmm eee` | SUBA/CMPA/ADDA; `s=0/1` selects a word/long source, with every source EA legal. |
| `0000 ooo 0 ss mmm eee` | SUBI/ADDI/CMPI for `ooo=010/011/110`; data-alterable destinations only. |
| `0101 qqq d ss mmm eee` | ADDQ/SUBQ for `d=0/1`; `qqq=000` means eight, otherwise one through seven. An accepts word/long only. |
| `oooo ddd 1 ss 00 m rrr` | SUBX/ADDX for `oooo=1001/1101`; `m=0` selects Dn pairs, `m=1` selects predecrement An pairs. |
| `1011 ddd 1 ss 001 rrr` | CMPM compares two postincrement memory operands. |
| `0100 0n00 ss mmm eee` | NEGX/NEG for `n=0/1`; data-alterable destinations only. |

Every family receives the raw low `mode/code` fields and `upperCode`, the
three bits at 11–9. The chapter binds ordinary register operands and explicitly
assigns the paired-memory modes; `upperCode` supplies a quick amount or the
second address register in a memory pair. Wildcard `x` bits share a body rather
than generating one per literal or memory address mode. ADD/SUB/CMP immediate
source-EA encodings share bodies with their equivalent ADDI/SUBI/CMPI encodings.

### Arithmetic results and flags

The result sources below calculate from captured numbers and publish their
flags before returning the result for writeback. They perform no operand reads.
Ordinary operations do not read any old flag. Extended operations read Z then X
only after both operands have arrived. They first publish N/Z/V/C/X, then set Z
again to `old Z AND result-is-zero`; both stages remain explicit for partial
failure observation. T/S are preserved throughout.

The carry/borrow and overflow predicates use the original inputs and incoming
X, not a prematurely wrapped intermediate. Thus subtracting `$FF` plus X from
a byte can borrow even though the combined subtrahend would wrap to zero.
Negation uses subtraction from zero and the same result policies.

```cpu
policy addByteFlags "68000 add 8-bit flags" (left: 8, right: 8, result: 8, incoming: flag) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = addOverflow(left, right, incoming)
  C = carry(left, right, incoming)
  X = carry(left, right, incoming)
}
```

```cpu
source addByte "8-bit add and flags" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  result = add(left, right)
  apply addByteFlags(left, right, result, 0)
  return result
}
```

```cpu
policy subtractByteFlags "68000 subtract 8-bit flags" (left: 8, right: 8, result: 8, incoming: flag) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = overflow(left, right, incoming)
  C = borrow(left, right, incoming)
  X = borrow(left, right, incoming)
}
```

```cpu
source subtractByte "8-bit subtract and flags" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  result = subtract(left, right)
  apply subtractByteFlags(left, right, result, 0)
  return result
}
```

```cpu
policy compareByteFlags "68000 compare 8-bit flags" (left: 8, right: 8, result: 8, incoming: flag) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = overflow(left, right, incoming)
  C = borrow(left, right, incoming)
}
```

```cpu
source compareByte "8-bit compare and flags" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  result = subtract(left, right)
  apply compareByteFlags(left, right, result, 0)
  return result
}
```

```cpu
policy cumulativeByteZero "68000 cumulative zero" (previous: flag, result: 8) {
  Z = and(previous, zero(result))
}
```

```cpu
source addExtendedByte "8-bit add with X and cumulative Z" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  previousZero = flag Z
  incoming = flag X
  result = add(left, right, incoming)
  apply addByteFlags(left, right, result, incoming)
  apply cumulativeByteZero(previousZero, result)
  return result
}
```

```cpu
source subtractExtendedByte "8-bit subtract with X and cumulative Z" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  previousZero = flag Z
  incoming = flag X
  result = subtract(left, right, incoming)
  apply subtractByteFlags(left, right, result, incoming)
  apply cumulativeByteZero(previousZero, result)
  return result
}
```

```cpu
source negateByte "8-bit negate" (original: 8) : 8 {
  result = source subtractByte(u8(0), original)
  return result
}
```

```cpu

```

Address addition and subtraction are pure 32-bit calculations. The family
sign-extends a word EA before calling them; quick constants stay positive.
CMPA instead uses the long comparison source and its N/Z/V/C policy.

```cpu
source addAddress "32-bit address add without flags" (left: 32, right: 32) : 32 {
  return add(left, right)
}
```

```cpu
source subtractAddress "32-bit address subtract without flags" (left: 32, right: 32) : 32 {
  return subtract(left, right)
}
```

### Operand access and writeback

Read the complete source before selecting or resolving the destination. An A7
destination captures its bank before pending address updates commit and retains
that identity through writeback. Read its value after the commit, so an aliased
source's auto-update is visible to the arithmetic. Word address sources are
sign-extended; word and long quick operations on An both keep the full pointer.

Reject odd word/long addresses before committing either operand's pending
updates. Commit before reading the destination. Source failures discard pending
updates; destination-read failures retain them. Apply result flags before any
writeback, preserving the destination's live upper Dn bits for byte/word writes.
A failed write retains flags, committed updates, and earlier bytes. Comparisons
never write a destination. Memory remains high byte first, with 32-bit logical
wrap and separate PC-relative program-space accesses.

The paired forms resolve the source first and the destination second, using
literal predecrement or postincrement modes. The chapter decoder makes the first
pending value available to the second resolution, including when both name the
same An; the families commit only after both alignment checks succeed.

### Byte arithmetic families

Capture the source Dn. Read the destination after the source. Extended
calculations capture Z then X and accumulate zero. Apply N/Z/V/C and copy
carry/borrow to X before writeback. Preserve T/S and the live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic8ModifyDataData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1101 ddd 0 00 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = addByte named "ADD.B {r},{d}"
  encoding "1101 ddd 1 00 00 0 rrr" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = addExtendedByte named "ADDX.B {r},{d}"
  encoding "1001 ddd 0 00 000 rrr" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = subtractByte named "SUB.B {r},{d}"
  encoding "1001 ddd 1 00 00 0 rrr" for d in dataRegisterCodes, r in dataRegisters with calculation = subtractExtendedByte named "SUBX.B {r},{d}"
  originalSource = operand r
  sourceValue = truncate(originalSource, 8)
  registerCode = operand d
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Read the complete data-space source. Commit pending addresses before reading the
destination. Apply N/Z/V/C and copy carry/borrow to X before writeback. Preserve
T/S and the live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic8ModifyMemoryData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1101 ddd 0 00 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = addByte named "ADD.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1101 ddd 0 00 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = addByte named "ADD.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1101 ddd 0 00 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = addByte named "ADD.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1101 ddd 0 00 111 00x" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = addByte named "ADD.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1001 ddd 0 00 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = subtractByte named "SUB.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1001 ddd 0 00 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = subtractByte named "SUB.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1001 ddd 0 00 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = subtractByte named "SUB.B MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1001 ddd 0 00 111 00x" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = subtractByte named "SUB.B MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  sourceValue = source readMemoryByte(sourceAddress)
  registerCode = operand d
  commit addresses
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve and read the first predecrement operand. Resolve the second operand.
Commit pending addresses before reading the destination. Extended calculations
capture Z then X and accumulate zero. Apply N/Z/V/C and copy carry/borrow to X
before writeback. Preserve T/S.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic8ModifyPredecrementPair (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {

  encoding "1001 xxx 1 00 00 1 xxx" with calculation = subtractExtendedByte named "SUBX.B MEMORY,MEMORY"

  sourceValue = source readMemoryByte(sourceAddress)
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, u3(4), upperCode)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
}
```

```cpu
family operandArithmetic8ModifyUnaryData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 00 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = negateByte named "NEG.B {d}"
  encoding "0100 0000 00 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = negateExtendedByte named "NEGX.B {d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalDestination, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve the destination. Commit pending addresses before reading the
destination. Extended calculations capture Z then X and accumulate zero. Apply
N/Z/V/C and copy carry/borrow to X before writeback. Preserve T/S.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic8ModifyUnaryMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 00 01x xxx" with calculation = negateByte named "NEG.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0100 00 10x xxx" with calculation = negateByte named "NEG.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0100 00 110 xxx" with calculation = negateByte named "NEG.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0100 00 111 00x" with calculation = negateByte named "NEG.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 00 01x xxx" with calculation = negateExtendedByte named "NEGX.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 00 10x xxx" with calculation = negateExtendedByte named "NEGX.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 00 110 xxx" with calculation = negateExtendedByte named "NEGX.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 00 111 00x" with calculation = negateExtendedByte named "NEGX.B MEMORY"
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  result = source calculation(destination)
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
```

### Word arithmetic families

calculations capture Z then X and accumulate zero. Apply N/Z/V/C and copy
carry/borrow to X before writeback. Preserve T/S and the live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
  destinationBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  destination = source readAddressRegister(destinationBank)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
}
```

Select and read the source An bank. Select the destination bank, retaining it
through the instruction. Read the destination after the source. Apply N/Z/V/C,
preserve X/T/S, and omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32CompareAddressAddress (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 001 rrr" for d in addressRegisters, r in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L {r},{d}"
  sourceCode = operand r
  sourceBank = source selectAddressRegister(sourceCode)
  originalSource = source readAddressRegister(sourceBank)
  sourceValue = originalSource
  registerCode = operand d
  destinationBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  destination = source readAddressRegister(destinationBank)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
}
```

Read the complete data-space source. Select the destination bank, retaining it
through the instruction. Reject odd word/long addresses before committing
updates. Commit pending addresses before reading the destination. Apply N/Z/V/C,
preserve X/T/S, and omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32CompareMemoryAddress (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 01x xxx" for d in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 10x xxx" for d in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 110 xxx" for d in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 111 00x" for d in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(32, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceValue = source readMemoryLong(sourceAddress)
  registerCode = operand d
  destinationBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readAddressRegister(destinationBank)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
}
```

Read the complete program-space source. Select the destination bank, retaining
it through the instruction. Reject odd word/long addresses before committing
updates. Commit pending addresses before reading the destination. Apply N/Z/V/C,
preserve X/T/S, and omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32CompareProgramAddress (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 111 01x" for d in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L PROGRAM,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(32, mode, code)
  fault alignment program read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceValue = source readProgramLong(sourceAddress)
  registerCode = operand d
  destinationBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readAddressRegister(destinationBank)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
}
```

Fetch the complete immediate. Select the destination bank, retaining it through
the instruction. Read the destination after the source. Apply N/Z/V/C, preserve
X/T/S, and omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32CompareImmediateAddress (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1011 ddd 111 111 100" for d in addressRegisters with calculation = compareLong named "CMPA.L IMMEDIATE,{d}"
  sourceValue = source immediateLong
  registerCode = operand d
  destinationBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  destination = source readAddressRegister(destinationBank)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
}
```

Resolve and read the first postincrement operand. Resolve the second operand.
Reject odd word/long addresses before committing updates. Commit pending
addresses before reading the destination. Apply N/Z/V/C, preserve X/T/S, and
omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32ComparePostincrementPair (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1011 xxx 1 10 001 xxx" with calculation = compareLong named "CMPM.L MEMORY,MEMORY"
  sourceAddress = resolve(32, u3(3), code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceValue = source readMemoryLong(sourceAddress)
  destinationAddress = resolve(32, u3(3), upperCode)
  fault alignment read(destinationAddress) if lowBit(destinationAddress)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readMemoryLong(destinationAddress)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
}
```

Read the original destination. Extended calculations capture Z then X and
accumulate zero. Apply N/Z/V/C and copy carry/borrow to X before writeback.
Preserve T/S.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32ModifyUnaryData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 10 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = negateLong named "NEG.L {d}"
  encoding "0100 0000 10 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = negateExtendedLong named "NEGX.L {d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  originalDestination = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = originalDestination
  result = source calculation(destination)
  perform writeDataLong(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve the destination. Reject odd word/long addresses before committing
updates. Commit pending addresses before reading the destination. Extended
calculations capture Z then X and accumulate zero. Apply N/Z/V/C and copy
carry/borrow to X before writeback. Preserve T/S.

```cpu
family operandArithmetic32ModifyUnaryMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 10 01x xxx" with calculation = negateLong named "NEG.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0100 10 10x xxx" with calculation = negateLong named "NEG.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0100 10 110 xxx" with calculation = negateLong named "NEG.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0100 10 111 00x" with calculation = negateLong named "NEG.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 10 01x xxx" with calculation = negateExtendedLong named "NEGX.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 10 10x xxx" with calculation = negateExtendedLong named "NEGX.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 10 110 xxx" with calculation = negateExtendedLong named "NEGX.L MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 10 111 00x" with calculation = negateExtendedLong named "NEGX.L MEMORY"
  destinationAddress = resolve(32, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(destinationAddress) if lowBit(destinationAddress)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readMemoryLong(destinationAddress)
  result = source calculation(destination)
  perform writeMemoryLong(destinationAddress, result)
}
```

## Bits, shifts, and rotates

Bit operations test or change one bit, shifts move a fixed-width value, and TAS
combines a byte test with setting its top bit. Their encodings keep the operation,
count source, width, and destination roles visible:

| Pattern | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `0000 bbb 1 oo mmm rrr` | Dynamic bit number in Dbbb; `oo=00/01/10/11` selects BTST/BCHG/BCLR/BSET. |
| `0000 1000 oo mmm rrr` | Immediate bit number in an extension word, fetched before destination extensions. |
| `1110 ccc d ss i tt rrr` | Register shift/rotate: `d=0/1` right/left, `ss=00/01/10` byte/word/long, `tt=00/01/10/11` AS/LS/ROX/RO. |
| `1110 0 tt d 11 mmm rrr` | Shift or rotate an alterable memory word once; bit 11 remains zero on the original 68000. |
| `0100 1010 11 mmm rrr` | TAS on a data-alterable byte; the excluded immediate slot is ILLEGAL. |

A register shift's `i=0` takes its count from `ccc`, with zero encoding eight;
`i=1` reads Dccc and keeps its low six bits, permitting zero through 63. Capture
that register before reading the destination, even when both names are the same.
Bit numbers wrap modulo 32 for a data-register destination and modulo 8 for all
other operands. Mode `001` belongs to MOVEP, not to these bit operations.

BTST alone admits PC-relative operands. Dynamic BTST also admits an immediate
byte destination; the static form does not. Modifying bit operations and TAS
require a data register or alterable memory. All families below receive raw
`mode/code/upperCode` fields, as arithmetic does. The chapter binds register
roles and uses `upperCode` for quick counts; native code does not classify forms.

### Testing and changing a bit

The mask sources reduce the captured bit number before shifting a single one.
The result sources update only Z from the **original** operand and mask, then
return the tested or changed value. BTST families omit writeback altogether.
No bit operation reads X or changes N/V/C/X/T/S.

```cpu
source bitMaskByte "one selected bit in 8 bits" (bitNumber: 8) : 8 {
  mask = iterate(and(bitNumber, u8(7)), u8(1)) {
    return shiftLeft(mask, 0)
  }
  return mask
}
policy testedByteBit "68000 tested bit" (original: 8, mask: 8) {
  Z = zero(and(original, mask))
}
```

```cpu
source testBitByte "test a selected bit and set Z" (original: 8, bitNumber: 8) : 8 {
  mask = source bitMaskByte(bitNumber)
  apply testedByteBit(original, mask)
  return original
}
```

```cpu
source changeBitByte "change a selected bit and set Z" (original: 8, bitNumber: 8) : 8 {
  mask = source bitMaskByte(bitNumber)
  apply testedByteBit(original, mask)
  return xor(original, mask)
}
```

```cpu
source clearBitByte "clear a selected bit and set Z" (original: 8, bitNumber: 8) : 8 {
  mask = source bitMaskByte(bitNumber)
  apply testedByteBit(original, mask)
  return and(original, xor(mask, u8($FF)))
}
```

```cpu
source setBitByte "set a selected bit and set Z" (original: 8, bitNumber: 8) : 8 {
  mask = source bitMaskByte(bitNumber)
  apply testedByteBit(original, mask)
  return or(original, mask)
}
```

```cpu
source bitMaskLong "one selected bit in 32 bits" (bitNumber: 8) : 32 {
  mask = iterate(and(bitNumber, u8(31)), u32(1)) {
    return shiftLeft(mask, 0)
  }
  return mask
}
policy testedLongBit "68000 tested bit" (original: 32, mask: 32) {
  Z = zero(and(original, mask))
}
```

```cpu
source testBitLong "test a selected bit and set Z" (original: 32, bitNumber: 8) : 32 {
  mask = source bitMaskLong(bitNumber)
  apply testedLongBit(original, mask)
  return original
}
```

```cpu

  apply testedLongBit(original, mask)
  return xor(original, mask)
}
```

```cpu

  apply testedLongBit(original, mask)
  return and(original, xor(mask, u32($FFFFFFFF)))
}
```

```cpu
source setBitLong "set a selected bit and set Z" (original: 32, bitNumber: 8) : 32 {
  mask = source bitMaskLong(bitNumber)
  apply testedLongBit(original, mask)
  return or(original, mask)
}
```

TAS sets N/Z and clears V/C from the old byte **before** setting bit 7. It
preserves X/T/S. The instruction-level model retains the ordered read and write;
it does not model a bus arbitration lock or cycle timing.

```cpu
source testAndSetByte "test the old byte then set its top bit" (original: 8) : 8 {
  apply byteResult(original)
  return or(original, u8($80))
}
```

### Local shift state

Each iteration moves one bit. `shifted`, `extendBit`, `carryBit`, and `overflowBit`
are typed local values, not CPU registers or flags. Every `next` expression sees
the same old locals; all four updates become visible together for the next step.
Zero iterations retain the initial values. Only the final policy publishes
N/Z/V/C/X, before writeback. Capture X after the operand read, even for ordinary
rotates whose final X retains that captured value.

| Operation | Incoming bit | Outgoing bit and flag behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ASL/LSL | Zero | Old sign bit goes to C/X; ASL accumulates every sign change in V. |
| ASR | Old sign | Old low bit goes to C/X, preserving the sign through each step. |
| LSR | Zero | Old low bit goes to C/X. |
| ROXL/ROXR | Previous local X | Outgoing bit becomes both C and the next local X. |
| ROL/ROR | Outgoing bit | Outgoing bit becomes C; local X is unchanged. |

All shifts set N/Z from the final value. Only ASL can set V; every other form
clears it. At count zero, ROX copies captured X to C, the other families clear C,
and all retain X and leave the numeric result unchanged. Register writeback
still occurs, preserving live upper Dn bits for byte/word widths.

```cpu
policy shiftByteFlags "68000 shift result" (result: 8, extendBit: flag, carryBit: flag, overflowBit: flag) {
  N = negative(result)
  Z = zero(result)
  V = overflowBit
  C = carryBit
  X = extendBit
}
```

```cpu
source asrByte "ASR with local carry, X, and overflow" (original: 8, count: 8) : 8 {
  initialExtend = flag X
  iterate(count) {
    shifted: 8 = original
    extendBit: flag = initialExtend
    carryBit: flag = 0
    overflowBit: flag = 0
    step {
      result = shiftRight(shifted, negative(shifted))
      next shifted = result
      next extendBit = lowBit(shifted)
      next carryBit = lowBit(shifted)
      next overflowBit = 0
    }
  }
  apply shiftByteFlags(shifted, extendBit, carryBit, overflowBit)
  return shifted
}
```

```cpu
source aslByte "ASL with local carry, X, and overflow" (original: 8, count: 8) : 8 {
  initialExtend = flag X
  iterate(count) {
    shifted: 8 = original
    extendBit: flag = initialExtend
    carryBit: flag = 0
    overflowBit: flag = 0
    step {
      result = shiftLeft(shifted, 0)
      next shifted = result
      next extendBit = negative(shifted)
      next carryBit = negative(shifted)
      next overflowBit = or(overflowBit, xor(negative(shifted), negative(result)))
    }
  }
  apply shiftByteFlags(shifted, extendBit, carryBit, overflowBit)
  return shifted
}
```

```cpu
      next carryBit = negative(shifted)
      next overflowBit = 0
    }
  }
  apply shiftLongFlags(shifted, extendBit, carryBit, overflowBit)
  return shifted
}
```

```cpu
source rorLong "ROR with local carry, X, and overflow" (original: 32, count: 8) : 32 {
  initialExtend = flag X
  iterate(count) {
    shifted: 32 = original
    extendBit: flag = initialExtend
    carryBit: flag = 0
    overflowBit: flag = 0
    step {
      result = shiftRight(shifted, lowBit(shifted))
      next shifted = result
      next extendBit = extendBit
      next carryBit = lowBit(shifted)
      next overflowBit = 0
    }
  }
  apply shiftLongFlags(shifted, extendBit, carryBit, overflowBit)
  return shifted
}
```

```cpu
source rolLong "ROL with local carry, X, and overflow" (original: 32, count: 8) : 32 {
  initialExtend = flag X
  iterate(count) {
    shifted: 32 = original
    extendBit: flag = initialExtend
    carryBit: flag = 0
    overflowBit: flag = 0
    step {
      result = shiftLeft(shifted, negative(shifted))
      next shifted = result
      next extendBit = extendBit
      next carryBit = negative(shifted)
      next overflowBit = 0
    }
  }
  apply shiftLongFlags(shifted, extendBit, carryBit, overflowBit)
  return shifted
}
```

### Bit-operation families

Capture the bit-number register or immediate extension before resolving and
reading the tested operand. Memory forms commit pending address updates before
the read, including PC-relative BTST. Byte operands allow odd addresses. Only Z
changes; modifying forms write afterward. A failed read retains committed
updates and old flags; a failed write retains Z and any completed bytes.

Capture the bit-number Dn before reading the full destination Dn. Set only Z
from the original bit; omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandBitsDynamicTestData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0000 bbb 1 00 000 ddd" for b in dataRegisters, d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = testBitLong named "BTST.L {b},{d}"
  numberRegister = operand b
  bitNumber = truncate(numberRegister, 8)
  registerCode = operand d
  original = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  result = source calculation(original, bitNumber)
}
```

Capture the bit-number Dn before resolving, committing updates, and reading the
data byte. Set only Z from the original bit; omit writeback.

```cpu
family operandBitsDynamicTestMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0000 bbb 1 00 01x xxx" for b in dataRegisters with calculation = testBitByte named "BTST.B {b},MEMORY"
  encoding "0000 bbb 1 00 10x xxx" for b in dataRegisters with calculation = testBitByte named "BTST.B {b},MEMORY"
  encoding "0000 bbb 1 00 110 xxx" for b in dataRegisters with calculation = testBitByte named "BTST.B {b},MEMORY"
  encoding "0000 bbb 1 00 111 00x" for b in dataRegisters with calculation = testBitByte named "BTST.B {b},MEMORY"
  numberRegister = operand b
  bitNumber = truncate(numberRegister, 8)
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  commit addresses
  original = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  result = source calculation(original, bitNumber)
}
```

program-space byte. Set only Z from the original bit; omit writeback.

```cpu

}
```

```cpu

```

### Shift and rotate families

All register forms capture the count before the destination. Immediate count
bits are runtime parameters shared by the eight encodings of one body. Memory
forms resolve a word address, reject odd alignment before committing updates,
then read the old word. Both bytes must arrive before X is captured or any flag
changes. Shift once and write the high byte before the low byte; a failed write
retains final flags, committed updates, and completed bytes.

Decode the positive quick count before reading the 8-bit destination. Capture X,
calculate using local iteration values, then apply final N/Z/V/C/X before
register writeback. Preserve the live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
family operandBitsShift8Quick (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1110 xxx 0 00 0 00 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = asrByte named "ASR.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 1 00 0 00 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = aslByte named "ASL.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 0 00 0 01 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = lsrByte named "LSR.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 1 00 0 01 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = lslByte named "LSL.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 0 00 0 10 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = roxrByte named "ROXR.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 1 00 0 10 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = roxlByte named "ROXL.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 0 00 0 11 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = rorByte named "ROR.B QUICK,{d}"
  encoding "1110 xxx 1 00 0 11 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with calculation = rolByte named "ROL.B QUICK,{d}"
  count = select(zero(upperCode), u8(8), extend(upperCode, 8))
  registerCode = operand d
  originalRegister = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  original = truncate(originalRegister, 8)
  result = source calculation(original, count)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Capture the count register and mask to six bits before reading the 8-bit
destination. Capture X, calculate using local iteration values, then apply final
N/Z/V/C/X before register writeback. Preserve the live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
family operandBitsShift8Register (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1110 ccc 0 00 1 00 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = asrByte named "ASR.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 1 00 1 00 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = aslByte named "ASL.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 0 00 1 01 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = lsrByte named "LSR.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 1 00 1 01 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = lslByte named "LSL.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 0 00 1 10 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = roxrByte named "ROXR.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 1 00 1 10 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = roxlByte named "ROXL.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 0 00 1 11 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = rorByte named "ROR.B {c},{d}"
  encoding "1110 ccc 1 00 1 11 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes, c in dataRegisters with calculation = rolByte named "ROL.B {c},{d}"
  countRegister = operand c
  count = and(truncate(countRegister, 8), u8(63))
  registerCode = operand d
  originalRegister = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  original = truncate(originalRegister, 8)
  result = source calculation(original, count)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```


