### Test and set families

Read the old byte, apply N/Z/V/C, then set bit 7 and write back. Dn writeback
preserves its live upper bits; memory forms commit pending updates before the
read. Preserve X/T/S. Failed writes retain the flags derived from the old byte.

```cpu
family operandBitsTasData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) "0100 1010 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "TAS.B {d}" {
  registerCode = operand d
  originalRegister = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  original = truncate(originalRegister, 8)
  result = source testAndSetByte(original)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve and commit pending address updates before reading the old byte. Apply
N/Z/V/C from that byte, set bit 7, and write; preserve X/T/S.

```cpu
family operandBitsTasMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1010 11 01x xxx" named "TAS.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1010 11 10x xxx" named "TAS.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1010 11 110 xxx" named "TAS.B MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1010 11 111 00x" named "TAS.B MEMORY"
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  commit addresses
  original = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  result = source testAndSetByte(original)
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
}
```

## Word products, division, and bounds

These instructions read a word source but use Dn differently: multiplication
replaces the entire register, division packs a word remainder above a word
quotient, and CHK tests only its low signed word.

| Pattern | Operation | Source and destination |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `1100 ddd s11 mmm rrr` | MULU/MULS | EA.W times Dddd.W gives Dddd.L; `s=0/1` unsigned/signed. |
| `1000 ddd s11 mmm rrr` | DIVU/DIVS | Dddd.L divided by EA.W; `s=0/1` unsigned/signed. |
| `0100 ddd 110 mmm rrr` | CHK | Test signed Dddd.W against the signed EA.W bound. |

Address-register direct sources are excluded. Data-register, memory,
PC-relative program-space, and immediate sources are distinct families below.
The whole source arrives before Dn is read. Odd word addresses fail before
reading data, changing flags, or committing staged address updates.

Unlike read/modify/write arithmetic, these families commit source auto-updates
**after** their result and flag effects. A zero-divisor or failed bound commits
those updates before returning its named exception request. A failed source
read discards pending updates. The native boundary still delivers exceptions.

### Products

Multiplication uses the low destination word, retains the complete 32-bit
product, and writes Dn before applying N/Z and clearing V/C. X is preserved.
The signed calculation interprets both words as two's-complement integers;
the resulting stored long retains the product's 32-bit representation.

```cpu
source unsignedWordProduct "unsigned word product" (left: 16, right: 16) : 32 {
  return multiply(left, right, unsigned)
}
source signedWordProduct "signed word product" (left: 16, right: 16) : 32 {
  return multiply(left, right, signed)
}
```

Read the complete source register word, then the destination. Write the full product before N/Z/V/C; preserve X.

```cpu
family operandWordProductData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 000 sss" for d in dataRegisters, s in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W {s},{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 000 sss" for d in dataRegisters, s in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W {s},{d}"
  sourceRegister = operand s
  sourceWord = truncate(sourceRegister, 16)
  destinationRegister = operand d
  result = source calculation(truncate(destinationRegister, 16), sourceWord)
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

Read the complete data-memory word after checking alignment, then the destination. Write the full product before N/Z/V/C; preserve X. Commit source updates last.

```cpu
family operandWordProductMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 111 00x" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 111 00x" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceWord = source readMemoryWord(sourceAddress)
  destinationRegister = operand d
  result = source calculation(truncate(destinationRegister, 16), sourceWord)
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
  commit addresses
}
```

Read the complete program-space word after checking alignment, then the destination. Write the full product before N/Z/V/C; preserve X. Commit source updates last.

```cpu
family operandWordProductProgram (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 111 01x" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W PROGRAM,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 111 01x" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W PROGRAM,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment program read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceWord = source readProgramWord(sourceAddress)
  destinationRegister = operand d
  result = source calculation(truncate(destinationRegister, 16), sourceWord)
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
  commit addresses
}
```

Read the complete immediate extension word, then the destination. Write the full product before N/Z/V/C; preserve X.

```cpu
family operandWordProductImmediate (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1100 ddd 011 111 100" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = unsignedWordProduct named "MULU.W IMMEDIATE,{d}"
  encoding "1100 ddd 111 111 100" for d in dataRegisters with calculation = signedWordProduct named "MULS.W IMMEDIATE,{d}"
  sourceWord = fetch word
  destinationRegister = operand d
  result = source calculation(truncate(destinationRegister, 16), sourceWord)
  operand d <- result
  apply longResult(result)
}
```

### Quotients and remainders

Clear C before examining the captured divisor. Zero requests `divide-by-zero`
without reading Dn, preserving N/Z/V/X. Otherwise, signed division truncates
toward zero and the remainder has the dividend's sign.

The third division result is a local Boolean, not an architectural flag.
`quotientOverflow` separates a nonzero divisor's oversized quotient from the
zero-divisor outcome. Overflow sets V and preserves Dn/N/Z/X. Success writes
`remainder:quotient`, then derives N/Z from the quotient and clears V/C.
The full signed range −32768 through 32767 is accepted. Both completed paths
commit source updates after their result/flag effects.

```cpu
policy divisionCarry "68000 division carry" () {
  C = 0
}
policy divisionOverflow "68000 division overflow" () {
  V = 1
}
```

Read the data source word. Clear C before testing for zero, which requests divide-by-zero without reading Dn. An unsigned quotient overflow sets only V; success writes remainder:quotient before N/Z/V/C. Preserve X.

```cpu
family operandWordDivuData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1000 ddd 011 000 sss" for d in dataRegisters, s in dataRegisters named "DIVU.W {s},{d}"
  sourceRegister = operand s
  sourceWord = truncate(sourceRegister, 16)
  apply divisionCarry()
  when zero(sourceWord) {
    reject "divide-by-zero"
  }
  dividend = operand d
  quotient, remainder, quotientOverflow = divide(dividend, sourceWord, unsigned) otherwise "divide-by-zero"
  when quotientOverflow {
    apply divisionOverflow()
  }
  when not(quotientOverflow) {
    operand d <- concat(remainder, quotient)
    apply wordResult(quotient)
  }
}
```

Read the memory source word. Clear C before testing for zero, which requests divide-by-zero without reading Dn. An unsigned quotient overflow sets only V; success writes remainder:quotient before N/Z/V/C. Preserve X. Commit source updates before a zero-divisor request, or after all successful/overflow effects.

```cpu
family operandWordDivuMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {

  encoding "1000 ddd 011 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisters named "DIVU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1000 ddd 011 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisters named "DIVU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "1000 ddd 011 111 00x" for d in dataRegisters named "DIVU.W MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceWord = source readMemoryWord(sourceAddress)
  apply divisionCarry()
  when zero(sourceWord) {
    commit addresses
    reject "divide-by-zero"
  }
  dividend = operand d
  quotient, remainder, quotientOverflow = divide(dividend, sourceWord, unsigned) otherwise "divide-by-zero"

    apply divisionOverflow()
  }
  when not(quotientOverflow) {
    operand d <- concat(remainder, quotient)
    apply wordResult(quotient)
  }
  commit addresses
}
```

Read the program source word. Clear C before testing for zero, which requests divide-by-zero without reading Dn. An unsigned quotient overflow sets only V; success writes remainder:quotient before N/Z/V/C. Preserve X. Commit source updates before a zero-divisor request, or after all successful/overflow effects.

### Signed bounds

CHK accepts a tested word from zero through the signed bound, inclusive, and
preserves all flags on success. A negative tested word fails with N set; a
nonnegative word above the bound fails with N clear. A negative bound accepts
nothing. On failure, preserve Z/V/C/X and request `bounds-check` after committing
any staged source updates. The explicit sign tests keep the unsigned borrow
comparison meaningful for the remaining nonnegative words.

```cpu
policy failedBound "68000 failed bound" (tested: 16) {
  N = negative(tested)
}
```

Read the data bound word before the destination. Accept a nonnegative tested word no greater than the signed bound without flag changes. Otherwise set N from the tested sign, preserve Z/V/C/X, and request bounds-check.

```cpu
family operandWordBoundsData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 ddd 110 000 sss" for d in dataRegisters, s in dataRegisters named "CHK.W {s},{d}"
  sourceRegister = operand s
  sourceWord = truncate(sourceRegister, 16)
  original = operand d
  tested = truncate(original, 16)
  when or(negative(tested), or(negative(sourceWord), borrow(sourceWord, tested))) {
    apply failedBound(tested)
    reject "bounds-check"
  }
}
```

Read the memory bound word before the destination. Accept a nonnegative tested word no greater than the signed bound without flag changes. Otherwise set N from the tested sign, preserve Z/V/C/X, and request bounds-check. Commit source updates on either completed path, before requesting an exception.

```cpu
family operandWordBoundsMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 ddd 110 01x xxx" for d in dataRegisters named "CHK.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "0100 ddd 110 10x xxx" for d in dataRegisters named "CHK.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "0100 ddd 110 110 xxx" for d in dataRegisters named "CHK.W MEMORY,{d}"
  encoding "0100 ddd 110 111 00x" for d in dataRegisters named "CHK.W MEMORY,{d}"
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  sourceWord = source readMemoryWord(sourceAddress)
  original = operand d
  tested = truncate(original, 16)
  when or(negative(tested), or(negative(sourceWord), borrow(sourceWord, tested))) {
    apply failedBound(tested)
    commit addresses
    reject "bounds-check"
  }
  commit addresses
}
```

Read the program bound word before the destination. Accept a nonnegative tested word no greater than the signed bound without flag changes. Otherwise set N from the tested sign, preserve Z/V/C/X, and request bounds-check. Commit source updates on either completed path, before requesting an exception.


## Packed decimal arithmetic

ABCD and SBCD add or subtract two packed decimal bytes with X as incoming
carry/borrow. NBCD subtracts one byte and X from zero. Each nibble represents
one decimal digit. Correct the low digit first, carry its carry/borrow into
the high digit, and combine the corrected nibbles.

| Pattern | Operation and operands |
| --- | --- |
| `1100 ddd 10000 m rrr` | ABCD; `m=0` Drrr,Dddd, `m=1` −(Arrr),−(Addd). |
| `1000 ddd 10000 m rrr` | SBCD, with the same register or paired-predecrement selection. |
| `0100 1000 00 mmm rrr` | NBCD on a data register or alterable memory byte. |

Capture X after reading both operands. Set C then X from the high digit's
carry/borrow. Only then read the previous Z and retain it if this result is
zero, allowing a multi-byte calculation to accumulate zero. Preserve N/V/T/S.
Flags precede destination writeback, even when the numeric result is unchanged.
The model applies the same deterministic correction to non-decimal nibbles;
these invalid-digit results are a model choice, not a portable software contract.

### Digit correction and flags

```cpu
policy decimalCarry "68000 decimal carry" (carry: flag) {
  C = carry
  X = carry
}
policy decimalZero "68000 decimal zero" (previous: flag, result: 8) {
  Z = and(previous, zero(result))
}
```

Add six to a digit sum above nine. Retain four corrected bits, feed the low carry/borrow into the high calculation, and publish final C/X before reading cumulative Z.

```cpu
source addDecimal "add packed decimal digits" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  incoming = flag X
  leftLow = and(left, u8(15))
  rightLow = and(right, u8(15))
  rawLow = add(leftLow, rightLow, incoming)
  digitLow = and(select(not(borrow(rawLow, u8(10))), add(rawLow, u8(6)), rawLow), u8(15))
  leftHigh = and(shiftBits(left, right, 4), u8(15))
  rightHigh = and(shiftBits(right, right, 4), u8(15))
  rawHigh = add(leftHigh, rightHigh, not(borrow(rawLow, u8(10))))
  digitHigh = and(select(not(borrow(rawHigh, u8(10))), add(rawHigh, u8(6)), rawHigh), u8(15))
  result = or(shiftBits(digitHigh, left, 4), digitLow)
  apply decimalCarry(not(borrow(rawHigh, u8(10))))
  previousZero = flag Z
  apply decimalZero(previousZero, result)
  return result
}
```

Subtract six when a digit subtraction borrows. Retain four corrected bits, feed the low carry/borrow into the high calculation, and publish final C/X before reading cumulative Z.

```cpu
source subtractDecimal "subtract packed decimal digits" (left: 8, right: 8) : 8 {
  incoming = flag X
  leftLow = and(left, u8(15))
  rightLow = and(right, u8(15))
  rawLow = subtract(leftLow, rightLow, incoming)
  digitLow = and(select(borrow(leftLow, rightLow, incoming), subtract(rawLow, u8(6)), rawLow), u8(15))
  leftHigh = and(shiftBits(left, right, 4), u8(15))
  rightHigh = and(shiftBits(right, right, 4), u8(15))
  rawHigh = subtract(leftHigh, rightHigh, borrow(leftLow, rightLow, incoming))
  digitHigh = and(select(borrow(leftHigh, rightHigh, borrow(leftLow, rightLow, incoming)), subtract(rawHigh, u8(6)), rawHigh), u8(15))
  result = or(shiftBits(digitHigh, left, 4), digitLow)
  apply decimalCarry(borrow(leftHigh, rightHigh, borrow(leftLow, rightLow, incoming)))
  previousZero = flag Z
  apply decimalZero(previousZero, result)
  return result
}
```

### Decimal operand families

Paired memory forms resolve and read the source before resolving the destination.
Both predecrements use mode 100, irrespective of the single encoded `m` bit.
Two uses of the same An therefore decrement twice; A7 steps by two per byte in
its selected stack bank. Commit all pending updates before reading the destination.
A failed source read discards updates. A failed destination read retains committed
updates, and a failed write also retains the new flags.

Read the source byte before the destination byte. Capture X, correct both digits, publish C/X and cumulative Z, then write the result while preserving live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
family operandDecimalPairData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1100 ddd 10000 0 sss" for d in dataRegisterCodes, s in dataRegisters with calculation = addDecimal named "ABCD {s},{d}"
  encoding "1000 ddd 10000 0 sss" for d in dataRegisterCodes, s in dataRegisters with calculation = subtractDecimal named "SBCD {s},{d}"
  sourceRegister = operand s
  sourceValue = truncate(sourceRegister, 8)
  registerCode = operand d
  destinationRegister = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(destinationRegister, 8)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve and read the source predecrement byte, then resolve the destination predecrement. Commit pending updates before reading the destination. Capture X, correct both digits, publish C/X and cumulative Z, then write the byte; preserve N/V/T/S.

```cpu
family operandDecimalPairMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "1100 xxx 10000 1 xxx" with calculation = addDecimal named "ABCD MEMORY,MEMORY"
  encoding "1000 xxx 10000 1 xxx" with calculation = subtractDecimal named "SBCD MEMORY,MEMORY"
  sourceAddress = resolve(8, u3(4), code)
  sourceValue = source readMemoryByte(sourceAddress)
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, u3(4), upperCode)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  result = source calculation(destination, sourceValue)
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
}
```

Read the original byte, then subtract it and X from zero using decimal correction. Publish C/X and cumulative Z before writeback; preserve N/V/T/S and live upper Dn bits.

```cpu
family operandDecimalNegateData (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1000 00 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "NBCD {d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  originalRegister = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  destination = truncate(originalRegister, 8)
  result = source subtractDecimal(u8(0), destination)
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve the byte destination and commit updates before its read. Subtract that byte and X from zero using decimal correction. Publish C/X and cumulative Z before writing the byte; preserve N/V/T/S.

```cpu
family operandDecimalNegateMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, upperCode: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1000 00 01x xxx" named "NBCD MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1000 00 10x xxx" named "NBCD MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1000 00 110 xxx" named "NBCD MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1000 00 111 00x" named "NBCD MEMORY"
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  commit addresses
  destination = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  result = source subtractDecimal(u8(0), destination)
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
}
```

## Conditions and control flow

The control instructions distinguish three addresses: the stored PC, the
sequential fetch cursor, and a selected target for retirement. Reading the
cursor neither fetches memory nor changes the target. Selecting a target does
not move the cursor. The core commits a selected target only when the
instruction
completes; the chapter decides when to validate it and when to stack a return
address. All address arithmetic wraps at 32 bits.

| Pattern | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `0101 cccc 11 mmm rrr` | Scc on a data-alterable byte; `mmm=001` instead selects DBcc Drrr. |
| `0110 cccc dddddddd` | BRA for `cccc=0000`, BSR for `0001`, otherwise Bcc. A zero displacement byte fetches a signed word; `$FF` means −1. |
| `0100 aaa 111 mmm rrr` | LEA of a control address into Aaaa. |
| `0100 1000 01 mmm rrr` | PEA, stacking the control address itself. |
| `0100 1110 10 mmm rrr` / `0100 1110 11 mmm rrr` | JSR / JMP to a control address. |
| `0100 1110 0101 u rrr` | LINK for `u=0`, UNLK for `u=1`. |
| `0100 1110 0111 0101` | RTS. |

Control addresses allow `(An)`, displacement, index, absolute, and PC-relative
forms. They calculate an address without reading its contents. LEA and PEA
can therefore carry odd addresses; taken jumps and returns require even
targets.

### Condition evaluation

In `cccc=tttp`, `ttt` chooses T, HI, CC, NE, VC, PL, GE, or GT, and `p=1`
inverts that test. T/F read no flags. Compound conditions capture C then Z,
N then V, or N then V then Z, including when an earlier flag already determines
the answer. These sources return a numeric Boolean while preserving every flag.

```cpu
source conditionT "68000 T condition" : 8 {
  return select(1, u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionF "68000 F condition" : 8 {
  return select(0, u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionHI "68000 HI condition" : 8 {
  c = flag C
  z = flag Z
  return select(not(or(c, z)), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionLS "68000 LS condition" : 8 {
  c = flag C
  z = flag Z
  return select(or(c, z), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionCC "68000 CC condition" : 8 {
  c = flag C
  return select(not(c), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionCS "68000 CS condition" : 8 {
  c = flag C
  return select(c, u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionNE "68000 NE condition" : 8 {
  z = flag Z
  return select(not(z), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionEQ "68000 EQ condition" : 8 {
  z = flag Z
  return select(z, u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionVC "68000 VC condition" : 8 {
  v = flag V
  return select(not(v), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionVS "68000 VS condition" : 8 {
  v = flag V
  return select(v, u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionPL "68000 PL condition" : 8 {
  n = flag N
  return select(not(n), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionMI "68000 MI condition" : 8 {
  n = flag N
  return select(n, u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionGE "68000 GE condition" : 8 {
  n = flag N
  v = flag V
  return select(not(xor(n, v)), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionLT "68000 LT condition" : 8 {
  n = flag N
  v = flag V
  return select(xor(n, v), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionGT "68000 GT condition" : 8 {
  n = flag N
  v = flag V
  z = flag Z
  return select(and(not(z), not(xor(n, v))), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

```cpu
source conditionLE "68000 LE condition" : 8 {
  n = flag N
  v = flag V
  z = flag Z
  return select(or(z, xor(n, v)), u8(1), u8(0))
}
```

### Setting a byte and decrementing a counter

Scc resolves and reads its destination before capturing the condition. Memory
auto-updates commit before that read. It writes `$FF` for true and zero for
false,
even if unchanged; register writes preserve the live upper 24 bits. DBcc
instead
captures the condition before its displacement word. A true condition leaves Dn
untouched. Otherwise decrement its low word; `$FFFF` falls through. A taken
DBcc
checks and selects its target before writing the counter, preserving live upper
bits. Both instructions preserve all flags.

Read Dn before testing the condition, then write the result byte while
preserving its live upper bits. Preserve every flag.

```cpu
family operandControlSetData (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0101 0000 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionT named "ST {d}"
  encoding "0101 0001 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionF named "SF {d}"
  encoding "0101 0010 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionHI named "SHI {d}"
  encoding "0101 0011 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionLS named "SLS {d}"
  encoding "0101 0100 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionCC named "SCC {d}"
  encoding "0101 0101 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionCS named "SCS {d}"
  encoding "0101 0110 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionNE named "SNE {d}"
  encoding "0101 0111 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionEQ named "SEQ {d}"
  encoding "0101 1000 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionVC named "SVC {d}"
  encoding "0101 1001 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionVS named "SVS {d}"
  encoding "0101 1010 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionPL named "SPL {d}"
  encoding "0101 1011 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionMI named "SMI {d}"
  encoding "0101 1100 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionGE named "SGE {d}"
  encoding "0101 1101 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionLT named "SLT {d}"
  encoding "0101 1110 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionGT named "SGT {d}"
  encoding "0101 1111 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionLE named "SLE {d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  previous = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  tested = source condition
  result = select(not(zero(tested)), u8($FF), u8(0))
  perform writeDataByte(registerCode, result)
}
```

Resolve the byte destination, commit updates, and read its old byte before
testing the condition. Always write the resulting byte, preserving every flag.

```cpu
family operandControlSetMemory (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0101 0000 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionT named "ST MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0000 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionT named "ST MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0000 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionT named "ST MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0000 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionT named "ST MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0001 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionF named "SF MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0001 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionF named "SF MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0001 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionF named "SF MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0001 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionF named "SF MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0010 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionHI named "SHI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0010 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionHI named "SHI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0010 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionHI named "SHI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0010 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionHI named "SHI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0011 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionLS named "SLS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0011 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionLS named "SLS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0011 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionLS named "SLS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0011 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionLS named "SLS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0100 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionCC named "SCC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0100 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionCC named "SCC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0100 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionCC named "SCC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0100 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionCC named "SCC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0101 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionCS named "SCS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0101 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionCS named "SCS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0101 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionCS named "SCS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0101 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionCS named "SCS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0110 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionNE named "SNE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0110 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionNE named "SNE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0110 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionNE named "SNE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0110 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionNE named "SNE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0111 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionEQ named "SEQ MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0111 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionEQ named "SEQ MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0111 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionEQ named "SEQ MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 0111 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionEQ named "SEQ MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1000 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionVC named "SVC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1000 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionVC named "SVC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1000 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionVC named "SVC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1000 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionVC named "SVC MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1001 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionVS named "SVS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1001 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionVS named "SVS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1001 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionVS named "SVS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1001 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionVS named "SVS MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1010 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionPL named "SPL MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1010 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionPL named "SPL MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1010 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionPL named "SPL MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1010 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionPL named "SPL MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1011 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionMI named "SMI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1011 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionMI named "SMI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1011 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionMI named "SMI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1011 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionMI named "SMI MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1100 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionGE named "SGE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1100 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionGE named "SGE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1100 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionGE named "SGE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1100 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionGE named "SGE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1101 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionLT named "SLT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1101 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionLT named "SLT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1101 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionLT named "SLT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1101 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionLT named "SLT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1110 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionGT named "SGT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1110 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionGT named "SGT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1110 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionGT named "SGT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1110 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionGT named "SGT MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1111 11 01x xxx" with condition = conditionLE named "SLE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1111 11 10x xxx" with condition = conditionLE named "SLE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1111 11 110 xxx" with condition = conditionLE named "SLE MEMORY"
  encoding "0101 1111 11 111 00x" with condition = conditionLE named "SLE MEMORY"
  destinationAddress = resolve(8, mode, code)
  commit addresses
  previous = source readMemoryByte(destinationAddress)
  tested = source condition
  result = select(not(zero(tested)), u8($FF), u8(0))
  perform writeMemoryByte(destinationAddress, result)
}
```

Capture the condition before fetching the displacement. On a false condition,
decrement only the low word; validate a taken target before changing Dn.

```cpu
family operandControlDecrement (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0101 0000 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionT named "DBT {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0001 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionF named "DBF {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0010 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionHI named "DBHI {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0011 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionLS named "DBLS {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0100 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionCC named "DBCC {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0101 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionCS named "DBCS {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0110 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionNE named "DBNE {d},label"
  encoding "0101 0111 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionEQ named "DBEQ {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1000 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionVC named "DBVC {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1001 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionVS named "DBVS {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1010 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionPL named "DBPL {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1011 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionMI named "DBMI {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1100 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionGE named "DBGE {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1101 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionLT named "DBLT {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1110 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionGT named "DBGT {d},label"
  encoding "0101 1111 11 001 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes with condition = conditionLE named "DBLE {d},label"
  tested = source condition
  base = next address
  offset = fetch word
  target = add(base, signExtend(offset, 32))
  when zero(tested) {
    registerCode = operand d
    counter = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
    result = subtract(truncate(counter, 16), u16(1))
    when not(zero(xor(result, u16($FFFF)))) {
      fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
      select target(target)
    }
    perform writeDataWord(registerCode, result)
  }
}
```

### Branches and subroutine calls

Branches capture their condition before reading the sequential cursor or an
extension word. The displacement is relative to the cursor before that
extension,
not the cursor after it. An untaken branch never validates its target. BSR
always
calls: capture the return cursor after any extension, then select the active
stack bank. Check the decremented stack address before the target, select the
target before writing the return long, and commit the original stack bank only
after all four writes succeed. Callback changes to S cannot redirect that
commit.

Capture the condition and pre-extension cursor. Sign-extend the nonzero
displacement byte. Only a taken branch validates and selects its target;
preserve all flags and registers.

```cpu
family operandControlBranchByte (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0110 0000 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionT named "BRA.B label" except "0110 0000 00000000"
  encoding "0110 0010 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionHI named "BHI.B label" except "0110 0010 00000000"
  encoding "0110 0011 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionLS named "BLS.B label" except "0110 0011 00000000"
  encoding "0110 0100 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionCC named "BCC.B label" except "0110 0100 00000000"
  encoding "0110 0101 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionCS named "BCS.B label" except "0110 0101 00000000"
  encoding "0110 0110 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionNE named "BNE.B label" except "0110 0110 00000000"
  encoding "0110 0111 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionEQ named "BEQ.B label" except "0110 0111 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1000 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionVC named "BVC.B label" except "0110 1000 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1001 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionVS named "BVS.B label" except "0110 1001 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1010 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionPL named "BPL.B label" except "0110 1010 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1011 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionMI named "BMI.B label" except "0110 1011 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1100 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionGE named "BGE.B label" except "0110 1100 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1101 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionLT named "BLT.B label" except "0110 1101 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1110 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionGT named "BGT.B label" except "0110 1110 00000000"
  encoding "0110 1111 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionLE named "BLE.B label" except "0110 1111 00000000"
  tested = source condition
  base = next address
  target = add(base, signExtend(displacement, 32))
  when not(zero(tested)) {
    fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
    select target(target)
  }
}
```

Sign-extend the nonzero displacement byte from the captured cursor. Capture the
return cursor, check the stack before the target, select the target, push the
return long, and commit the captured stack bank.

```cpu
family operandControlCallByte (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0110 0001 xxxxxxxx" with condition = conditionT named "BSR.B label" except "0110 0001 00000000"
  tested = source condition
  base = next address
  target = add(base, signExtend(displacement, 32))
  returnAddress = next address
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  stackAddress = subtract(stack, u32(4))
  fault alignment write(stackAddress) if lowBit(stackAddress)
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
  perform writeMemoryLong(stackAddress, returnAddress)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, stackAddress)
}
```

Capture the condition and pre-extension cursor. Fetch and sign-extend the
displacement word. Only a taken branch validates and selects its target;
preserve all flags and registers.

```cpu
family operandControlBranchWord (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0110 0000 00000000" with condition = conditionT named "BRA.W label"
  encoding "0110 0010 00000000" with condition = conditionHI named "BHI.W label"
  encoding "0110 0011 00000000" with condition = conditionLS named "BLS.W label"
  encoding "0110 0100 00000000" with condition = conditionCC named "BCC.W label"
  encoding "0110 0101 00000000" with condition = conditionCS named "BCS.W label"
  encoding "0110 0110 00000000" with condition = conditionNE named "BNE.W label"
  encoding "0110 0111 00000000" with condition = conditionEQ named "BEQ.W label"
  encoding "0110 1000 00000000" with condition = conditionVC named "BVC.W label"
  encoding "0110 1001 00000000" with condition = conditionVS named "BVS.W label"
  encoding "0110 1010 00000000" with condition = conditionPL named "BPL.W label"
  encoding "0110 1011 00000000" with condition = conditionMI named "BMI.W label"
  encoding "0110 1100 00000000" with condition = conditionGE named "BGE.W label"
  encoding "0110 1101 00000000" with condition = conditionLT named "BLT.W label"
  encoding "0110 1110 00000000" with condition = conditionGT named "BGT.W label"
  encoding "0110 1111 00000000" with condition = conditionLE named "BLE.W label"
  tested = source condition
  base = next address
  offset = fetch word
  target = add(base, signExtend(offset, 32))
  when not(zero(tested)) {
    fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
    select target(target)
  }
}
```

Fetch the displacement word after capturing its base cursor. Capture the return
cursor, check the stack before the target, select the target, push the return
long, and commit the captured stack bank.

```cpu
family operandControlCallWord (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0110 0001 00000000" with condition = conditionT named "BSR.W label"
  tested = source condition
  base = next address
  offset = fetch word
  target = add(base, signExtend(offset, 32))
  returnAddress = next address
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  stackAddress = subtract(stack, u32(4))
  fault alignment write(stackAddress) if lowBit(stackAddress)
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
  perform writeMemoryLong(stackAddress, returnAddress)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, stackAddress)
}
```

### Control addresses and frames

LEA selects its destination register bank before resolving the source. PEA
selects
the active stack after resolving the source, checks stack alignment, and pushes
the address without validating it as a target. JSR captures the return cursor
after
all EA extensions and follows BSR's stack-before-target validation and delayed
stack commit. JMP validates and selects the resolved target directly.

Select the destination register bank before resolving the control address.
Write that complete address without a data read or alignment check.

```cpu
family operandControlLea (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 aaa 111 010 xxx" for a in addressRegisters named "LEA control EA,{a}"
  encoding "0100 aaa 111 101 xxx" for a in addressRegisters named "LEA control EA,{a}"
  encoding "0100 aaa 111 110 xxx" for a in addressRegisters named "LEA control EA,{a}"
  encoding "0100 aaa 111 111 0xx" for a in addressRegisters named "LEA control EA,{a}"
  registerCode = operand a
  destinationBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  target = resolve(32, mode, code)
  perform writeAddressRegister(destinationBank, target)
}
```

Resolve the control address before selecting the stack bank. Check the
decremented stack, push the address high byte first, then commit the captured
pointer.

```cpu
family operandControlPea (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 1000 01 010 xxx" named "PEA control EA"
  encoding "0100 1000 01 101 xxx" named "PEA control EA"
  encoding "0100 1000 01 110 xxx" named "PEA control EA"
  encoding "0100 1000 01 111 0xx" named "PEA control EA"
  target = resolve(32, mode, code)
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  stackAddress = subtract(stack, u32(4))
  fault alignment write(stackAddress) if lowBit(stackAddress)
  perform writeMemoryLong(stackAddress, target)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, stackAddress)
}
```

Resolve the address and capture the return cursor. Check stack alignment before
target alignment; select the target before stacking and commit the original
stack bank after all writes.

```cpu
family operandControlJsr (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 1110 10 010 xxx" named "JSR control EA"
  encoding "0100 1110 10 101 xxx" named "JSR control EA"
  encoding "0100 1110 10 110 xxx" named "JSR control EA"
  encoding "0100 1110 10 111 0xx" named "JSR control EA"
  target = resolve(32, mode, code)
  returnAddress = next address
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  stackAddress = subtract(stack, u32(4))
  fault alignment write(stackAddress) if lowBit(stackAddress)
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
  perform writeMemoryLong(stackAddress, returnAddress)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, stackAddress)
}
```

Resolve the control address, validate its alignment, and select it as the
target. Do not read target memory or change the sequential cursor.

```cpu
family operandControlJmp (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 1110 11 010 xxx" named "JMP control EA"
  encoding "0100 1110 11 101 xxx" named "JMP control EA"
  encoding "0100 1110 11 110 xxx" named "JMP control EA"
  encoding "0100 1110 11 111 0xx" named "JMP control EA"
  target = resolve(32, mode, code)
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
}
```

LINK fetches its signed allocation word first, selects the frame register's
identity, and then selects the active stack bank. Check stack alignment before
reading the saved register. LINK A7 saves the decremented stack address itself.
After all writes succeed, set the frame register, then apply the allocation to
the captured stack address. UNLK reads the saved long through the frame
register
before updating either register: advance the captured stack bank, then restore
the frame register. Consequently UNLK A7 leaves the popped value in SP.

RTS captures the active stack, reads the complete return long, and validates
and
selects its target before advancing that captured stack by four. Failed reads
or
an odd target preserve the pointer. All these operations preserve every flag.

Fetch the allocation before selecting either register identity. After checking
stack alignment, save the frame value, write the new frame pointer, and
allocate from the captured stack address. LINK A7 saves the decremented
pointer.

```cpu
family operandControlLink (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0101 0 rrr" for r in addressRegisters named "LINK {r},#allocation"
  allocation = fetch word
  frameCode = operand r
  frameBank = source selectAddressRegister(frameCode)
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  stackAddress = subtract(stack, u32(4))
  fault alignment write(stackAddress) if lowBit(stackAddress)
  saved = choose zero(xor(frameCode, u3(7))) : 32 {
    then {
      return stackAddress
    }
    else {
      original = source readAddressRegister(frameBank)
      return original
    }
  }
  perform writeMemoryLong(stackAddress, saved)
  perform writeAddressRegister(frameBank, stackAddress)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, add(stackAddress, signExtend(allocation, 32)))
}
```

Select the frame register before the stack bank. Read the complete saved long
before advancing the captured stack and restoring the frame register; preserve
flags.

```cpu
family operandControlUnlink (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0101 1 rrr" for r in addressRegisters named "UNLK {r}"
  frameCode = operand r
  frameBank = source selectAddressRegister(frameCode)
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  frameAddress = source readAddressRegister(frameBank)
  fault alignment read(frameAddress) if lowBit(frameAddress)
  saved = source readMemoryLong(frameAddress)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, add(frameAddress, u32(4)))
  perform writeAddressRegister(frameBank, saved)
}
```

Capture the active stack and read the complete return long. Validate and select
the target before advancing the captured pointer; preserve flags.

```cpu
family operandControlReturn (mode: 3, code: 3, displacement: 8) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0101" named "RTS"
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  fault alignment read(stack) if lowBit(stack)
  target = source readMemoryLong(stack)
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, add(stack, u32(4)))
}
```

## Peripheral and multiple-register transfers

MOVEP and MOVEM both preserve every flag, but describe different bus sequences.
MOVEP moves a word or long through alternate byte addresses; MOVEM traverses a
register mask and transfers complete registers consecutively. Neither performs
an ALU destination read before a store.

### MOVEP: alternate byte addresses

`0000 ddd 1 t s 001 aaa` selects Dddd, Aaaa, direction `t` (zero loads Dn),
and size `s` (zero word, one long). Capture An before fetching the signed
16-bit displacement. Read or write high byte first at alternate addresses,
wrapping each logical address at 32 bits. Odd addresses are legal. Stores read
Dn after fetching the displacement; loads replace Dn only after every byte
arrives. A word load preserves its live upper word.

```cpu
source readPeripheralWord "read alternate bytes of a 16-bit value" (address: 32) : 16 {
  byte0 = memory(address)
  byte1 = memory(add(address, u32(2)))
  return concat(byte0, byte1)
}
```

```cpu
action writePeripheralWord "write alternate bytes of a 16-bit value" (address: 32, contents: 16) using memory {
  memory(address) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 8), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(2))) <- truncate(contents, 8)
}
```

Capture the address register before fetching the displacement. Transfer high
byte first at alternate addresses; odd addresses are legal. Read every byte
before writing Dn, preserving its live upper word for a word load. Preserve
every flag.

```cpu
family operandTransferPeripheralWordLoad (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0000 ddd 1 0 0 001 aaa" for d in dataRegisterCodes, a in addressRegisters named "MOVEP.W (d,{a}),{d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  baseCode = operand a
  baseBank = source selectAddressRegister(baseCode)
  base = source readAddressRegister(baseBank)
  displacementWord = fetch word
  address = add(base, signExtend(displacementWord, 32))
  result = source readPeripheralWord(address)
  perform writeDataWord(registerCode, result)
}
```

Capture the address register before fetching the displacement. Transfer high
byte first at alternate addresses; odd addresses are legal. Read Dn after the
fetch, then write the bytes without destination reads. Preserve every flag.

```cpu
family operandTransferPeripheralWordStore (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0000 ddd 1 1 0 001 aaa" for d in dataRegisterCodes, a in addressRegisters named "MOVEP.W {d},(d,{a})"
  registerCode = operand d
  baseCode = operand a
  baseBank = source selectAddressRegister(baseCode)
  base = source readAddressRegister(baseBank)
  displacementWord = fetch word
  address = add(base, signExtend(displacementWord, 32))
  original = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  perform writePeripheralWord(address, truncate(original, 16))
}
```

```cpu
source readPeripheralLong "read alternate bytes of a 32-bit value" (address: 32) : 32 {
  byte0 = memory(address)
  byte1 = memory(add(address, u32(2)))
  byte2 = memory(add(address, u32(4)))
  byte3 = memory(add(address, u32(6)))
  return concat(concat(byte0, byte1), concat(byte2, byte3))
}
```

```cpu
action writePeripheralLong "write alternate bytes of a 32-bit value" (address: 32, contents: 32) using memory {
  memory(address) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 24), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(2))) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 16), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(4))) <- truncate(shiftBits(contents, right, 8), 8)
  memory(add(address, u32(6))) <- truncate(contents, 8)
}
```

Capture the address register before fetching the displacement. Transfer high
byte first at alternate addresses; odd addresses are legal. Read every byte
before writing Dn, preserving its live upper word for a word load. Preserve
every flag.

```cpu
family operandTransferPeripheralLongLoad (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0000 ddd 1 0 1 001 aaa" for d in dataRegisterCodes, a in addressRegisters named "MOVEP.L (d,{a}),{d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  baseCode = operand a
  baseBank = source selectAddressRegister(baseCode)
  base = source readAddressRegister(baseBank)
  displacementWord = fetch word
  address = add(base, signExtend(displacementWord, 32))
  result = source readPeripheralLong(address)
  perform writeDataLong(registerCode, result)
}
```

Capture the address register before fetching the displacement. Transfer high
byte first at alternate addresses; odd addresses are legal. Read Dn after the
fetch, then write the bytes without destination reads. Preserve every flag.

```cpu
family operandTransferPeripheralLongStore (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0000 ddd 1 1 1 001 aaa" for d in dataRegisterCodes, a in addressRegisters named "MOVEP.L {d},(d,{a})"
  registerCode = operand d
  baseCode = operand a
  baseBank = source selectAddressRegister(baseCode)
  base = source readAddressRegister(baseBank)
  displacementWord = fetch word
  address = add(base, signExtend(displacementWord, 32))
  original = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  perform writePeripheralLong(address, original)
}
```

### MOVEM: register mask and order

`0100 1 d 00 1 s mmm rrr` selects direction, size, and effective address.
`d=0` stores; `d=1` loads. Fetch the register mask before any EA extensions.
Normal order is D0 through D7, then A0 through A7. Predecrement stores reverse
both register order and mask interpretation: bit zero selects A7 and bit
fifteen
selects D0. An empty list still resolves EA extensions, but checks no alignment
and updates no base. Otherwise check the first transfer's alignment before
accessing any listed register or memory byte.

Capture each source and select each A7 bank at its own turn. Loads select that
register identity before reading its bytes and write it only when the complete
value arrives. Word loads sign-extend to all 32 bits. PC-relative loads use
program space. Stores read the live register at its turn; storing the base
register sees its original value. Earlier successful transfers survive
failures.

The following internal storage number extends D0–D7 with A0–A6, USP, and SSP.
Selecting A7 reads S at the selected register's turn. This number is a local
binding, not another hardware register.

```cpu
source selectTransferRegister "select a MOVEM register's storage" (index: 8) : 8 {
  selected = choose borrow(index, u8(8)) : 8 {
    then {
      return index
    }
    else {
      addressCode = truncate(subtract(index, u8(8)), 3)
      bank = source selectAddressRegister(addressCode)
      return add(u8(8), bank)
    }
  }
  return selected
}
source readTransferRegister "read selected MOVEM storage" (selected: 8) : 32 {
  contents = match selected : 32 {
    case "00000rrr" for r in dataRegisters {
      original = operand r
      return original
    }
    case "00001rrr" for r in addressStorage {
      original = operand r
      return original
    }
    case "00010000" {
      original = register SSP
      return original
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
  return contents
}
action writeTransferRegister "write selected MOVEM storage" (selected: 8, contents: 32) {
  match selected {
    case "00000rrr" for r in dataRegisters {
      operand r <- contents
    }
    case "00001rrr" for r in addressStorage {
      operand r <- contents
    }
    case "00010000" {
      SSP <- contents
    }
    otherwise unsupported
  }
}
```

Visit selected registers in normal order, advancing only for a selected
transfer. Resolve the control EA once, including for an empty list. An empty
list captures its address but performs no alignment check or transfer. Preserve
every flag; failed transfers retain earlier complete registers and written
bytes.

```cpu
family operandTransferMultipleWordStoreMemory (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1 0 00 1 0 010 xxx" named "MOVEM.W list,MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1 0 00 1 0 101 xxx" named "MOVEM.W list,MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1 0 00 1 0 110 xxx" named "MOVEM.W list,MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 1 0 00 1 0 111 00x" named "MOVEM.W list,MEMORY"
  mask = fetch word
  initialAddress = resolve(32, mode, code)
  when not(zero(mask)) {
    firstAddress = initialAddress
    fault alignment write(firstAddress) if lowBit(firstAddress)
    iterate(u8(16)) {
      remaining: 16 = mask
      registerIndex: 8 = u8(0)
      address: 32 = initialAddress
      step {
        selected = and(remaining, u16(1))
        followingAddress = select(not(zero(selected)), add(address, u32(2)), address)
        when not(zero(selected)) {
          storage = source selectTransferRegister(registerIndex)
          original = source readTransferRegister(storage)
          perform writeMemoryWord(address, truncate(original, 16))
        }
        next remaining = shiftBits(remaining, right, 1)
        next registerIndex = add(registerIndex, u8(1))
        next address = followingAddress
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Predecrement stores use reversed register order and subtract before each
selected transfer. Commit the captured base bank after the whole list succeeds;
a postincrement update wins over a loaded base. An empty list captures its
address but performs no alignment check or transfer. Preserve every flag;
failed transfers retain earlier complete registers and written bytes.

```cpu
family operandTransferMultipleWordStorePredecrement (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1 0 00 1 0 100 rrr" for r in addressRegisters named "MOVEM.W list,-({r})"
  mask = fetch word
  baseCode = operand r
  baseBank = source selectAddressRegister(baseCode)
  initialAddress = source readAddressRegister(baseBank)
  when not(zero(mask)) {
    firstAddress = subtract(initialAddress, u32(2))
    fault alignment write(firstAddress) if lowBit(firstAddress)
    iterate(u8(16)) {
      remaining: 16 = mask
      registerIndex: 8 = u8(0)
      address: 32 = initialAddress
      step {
        selected = and(remaining, u16(1))
        followingAddress = select(not(zero(selected)), subtract(address, u32(2)), address)
        when not(zero(selected)) {
          storage = source selectTransferRegister(subtract(u8(15), registerIndex))
          original = source readTransferRegister(storage)
          perform writeMemoryWord(followingAddress, truncate(original, 16))
        }
        next remaining = shiftBits(remaining, right, 1)
        next registerIndex = add(registerIndex, u8(1))
        next address = followingAddress
      }
    }
    perform writeAddressRegister(baseBank, address)
  }
}
```

Visit selected registers in normal order, advancing only for a selected
transfer. Resolve the control EA once, including for an empty list. Word loads
sign-extend into the complete register. An empty list captures its address but
performs no alignment check or transfer. Preserve every flag; failed transfers
retain earlier complete registers and written bytes.

## Status and system instructions

These instructions expose the boundary between instruction effects and
exception
delivery. A privilege check reads S before any operand access. A rejected
instruction returns a named reason to the core, which chooses its vector, saved
PC, and frame, and handles trace and nested faults. The instruction itself does
not silently perform that delivery.

| Pattern | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `0000 ooo0 0 f 111100` | Immediate CCR/SR logic; `ooo=000/001/101` means OR/AND/XOR, `f=1` selects privileged SR. |
| `0100 0 ff0 11 mmm rrr` | `ff=00` SR to data-alterable EA, `10` EA to CCR, `11` EA to privileged SR; `01` is absent on the original chip. |
| `0100 1110 0110 d rrr` | An to USP for `d=0`, USP to An for `d=1`; both privileged. |
| `0100 1110 0111 0xxx` | RESET, NOP, STOP, RTE, reserved, RTS, TRAPV, RTR. |
| `0100 1110 0100 vvvv` | TRAP; the core adds the low nibble to vector 32. |
| `0100 1010 1111 1100` | ILLEGAL. |
| `1010 xxxx xxxx xxxx` / `1111 xxxx xxxx xxxx` | Software emulator lines A and F. |

### Immediate status logic

Capture old packed SR before fetching the complete immediate word, even for
CCR. Apply the chosen operation to that capture, ignoring reserved bits on
restoration. CCR preserves T, S, and the live interrupt mask. SR restores
X/N/Z/V/C
first, then T/S and the interrupt mask. Check SR privilege before both status
capture and operand fetch.

Capture packed SR before fetching the immediate word. Restore the calculated
condition codes while preserving live system flags and the interrupt mask.

```cpu
family operandSystemImmediateCCR (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0000 0000 0 0 111100" with calculation = orWord named "ORI #n,CCR"
  encoding "0000 0010 0 0 111100" with calculation = andWord named "ANDI #n,CCR"
  encoding "0000 1010 0 0 111100" with calculation = eorWord named "EORI #n,CCR"
  status = source SR
  immediate = fetch word
  result = source calculation(status, immediate)
  perform writeCCR(result)
}
```

Check privilege and capture old SR before fetching the immediate word. Restore
the calculated status, including system flags and the interrupt mask.

```cpu
family operandSystemImmediateSR (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0000 0000 0 1 111100" with calculation = orWord named "ORI #n,SR"
  encoding "0000 0010 0 1 111100" with calculation = andWord named "ANDI #n,SR"
  encoding "0000 1010 0 1 111100" with calculation = eorWord named "EORI #n,SR"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  status = source SR
  immediate = fetch word
  result = source calculation(status, immediate)
  perform writeSR(result)
}
```

### Moving status and USP

MOVE from SR is unprivileged on the original 68000. Resolve and align a memory
destination, commit its auto-updates, and read its old word before capturing
SR.
Write the captured status word, preserving live upper Dn bits. Source-to-status
moves instead read the complete word and restore status before committing
staged
address updates. Those updates retain their original stack bank even if S
changes.
A failed source leaves both status and pending updates untouched.

Read the destination register before capturing SR. Write the status word while
preserving its live upper word; this instruction is unprivileged on the
original chip.

```cpu
family operandSystemFromStatusData (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0000 11 000 ddd" for d in dataRegisterCodes named "MOVE SR,{d}"
  registerCode = operand d
  previous = source readDataRegister(registerCode)
  status = source SR
  perform writeDataWord(registerCode, status)
}
```

Resolve and align the destination, commit updates, and read its old word before
capturing SR. Write the status high byte first, retaining completed effects on
failure.

```cpu
family operandSystemFromStatusMemory (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0000 11 01x xxx" named "MOVE SR,MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 11 10x xxx" named "MOVE SR,MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 11 110 xxx" named "MOVE SR,MEMORY"
  encoding "0100 0000 11 111 00x" named "MOVE SR,MEMORY"
  destinationAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(destinationAddress) if lowBit(destinationAddress)
  commit addresses
  previous = source readMemoryWord(destinationAddress)
  status = source SR
  perform writeMemoryWord(destinationAddress, status)
}
```

Read the complete source word without a privilege check. Restore only the
condition codes before committing any staged source-address updates, retaining
their original stack bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToCCRData (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 11 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "MOVE {r},CCR"
  original = operand r
  status = truncate(original, 16)
  perform writeCCR(status)
}
```

Read the complete source word without a privilege check. Restore only the
condition codes before committing any staged source-address updates, retaining
their original stack bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToCCRMemory (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 11 01x xxx" named "MOVE MEMORY,CCR"
  encoding "0100 0100 11 10x xxx" named "MOVE MEMORY,CCR"
  encoding "0100 0100 11 110 xxx" named "MOVE MEMORY,CCR"
  encoding "0100 0100 11 111 00x" named "MOVE MEMORY,CCR"
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  status = source readMemoryWord(sourceAddress)
  perform writeCCR(status)
  commit addresses
}
```

Read the complete source word without a privilege check. Restore only the
condition codes before committing any staged source-address updates, retaining
their original stack bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToCCRProgram (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 11 111 01x" named "MOVE PROGRAM,CCR"
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment program read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  status = source readProgramWord(sourceAddress)
  perform writeCCR(status)
  commit addresses
}
```

Read the complete source word without a privilege check. Restore only the
condition codes before committing any staged source-address updates, retaining
their original stack bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToCCRImmediate (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0100 11 111 100" named "MOVE IMMEDIATE,CCR"
  status = fetch word
  perform writeCCR(status)
}
```

Check privilege before reading the source word. Restore full SR before
committing any staged source-address updates, retaining their original stack
bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToSRData (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0110 11 000 rrr" for r in dataRegisters named "MOVE {r},SR"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  original = operand r
  status = truncate(original, 16)
  perform writeSR(status)
}
```

Check privilege before reading the source word. Restore full SR before
committing any staged source-address updates, retaining their original stack
bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToSRMemory (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0110 11 01x xxx" named "MOVE MEMORY,SR"
  encoding "0100 0110 11 10x xxx" named "MOVE MEMORY,SR"
  encoding "0100 0110 11 110 xxx" named "MOVE MEMORY,SR"
  encoding "0100 0110 11 111 00x" named "MOVE MEMORY,SR"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  status = source readMemoryWord(sourceAddress)
  perform writeSR(status)
  commit addresses
}
```

Check privilege before reading the source word. Restore full SR before
committing any staged source-address updates, retaining their original stack
bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToSRProgram (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0110 11 111 01x" named "MOVE PROGRAM,SR"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  sourceAddress = resolve(16, mode, code)
  fault alignment program read(sourceAddress) if lowBit(sourceAddress)
  status = source readProgramWord(sourceAddress)
  perform writeSR(status)
  commit addresses
}
```

Check privilege before reading the source word. Restore full SR before
committing any staged source-address updates, retaining their original stack
bank. A failed source leaves status unchanged.

```cpu
family operandSystemToSRImmediate (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 0110 11 111 100" named "MOVE IMMEDIATE,SR"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  status = fetch word
  perform writeSR(status)
}
```

USP transfers check privilege before selecting An's identity. Capture the
complete source before writing the destination; A7 selects the current stack
bank, normally SSP after the privilege check. Preserve every flag.

Check privilege, select and read the address register, then write USP. Preserve
every flag.

```cpu
family operandSystemToUserStack (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0110 0 rrr" for r in addressRegisters named "MOVE {r},USP"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  registerCode = operand r
  addressBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  contents = source readAddressRegister(addressBank)
  USP <- contents
}
```

Check privilege, select the address-register identity, then read USP and write
the captured destination. Preserve every flag.

```cpu
family operandSystemFromUserStack (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0110 1 rrr" for r in addressRegisters named "MOVE USP,{r}"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  registerCode = operand r
  addressBank = source selectAddressRegister(registerCode)
  contents = register USP
  perform writeAddressRegister(addressBank, contents)
}
```

### Returns, stopping, and device reset

RTE checks privilege, captures SSP, and reads PC high, SR, then PC low from
that
original supervisor stack. RTR captures the active stack and reads CCR before
the complete PC long. Both reject odd stack addresses before reading, then
validate and select the target before advancing the captured pointer by six
and restoring status. Neither a failed read nor an odd target changes the
pointer
or status. Restoring S never redirects reads already in progress.

STOP checks privilege, fetches a complete status word, restores SR, then halts.
RESET checks privilege before asserting the device reset connection; the core
records a successful signal, while callback failure stops retirement. NOP has
no effects after opcode fetching. TRAPV requests delivery only when V is set.

Check privilege and capture SSP. Read PC high, SR, then PC low. Validate and
select the target before advancing SSP and restoring the captured status.

```cpu
family operandSystemReturnException (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0011" named "RTE"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  stack = register SSP
  fault alignment read(stack) if lowBit(stack)
  high = source readMemoryWord(add(stack, u32(2)))
  status = source readMemoryWord(stack)
  low = source readMemoryWord(add(stack, u32(4)))
  target = concat(high, low)
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
  SSP <- add(stack, u32(6))
  perform writeSR(status)
}
```

Capture the active stack, read CCR then the PC long, and validate the target.
Advance the original stack bank before restoring condition codes; preserve
system status.

```cpu
family operandSystemReturnCondition (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0111" named "RTR"
  stackBank = source selectAddressRegister(u3(7))
  stack = source readAddressRegister(stackBank)
  fault alignment read(stack) if lowBit(stack)
  status = source readMemoryWord(stack)
  target = source readMemoryLong(add(stack, u32(2)))
  fault alignment fetch(target) if lowBit(target)
  select target(target)
  perform writeAddressRegister(stackBank, add(stack, u32(6)))
  perform writeCCR(status)
}
```

Check privilege before signaling device reset. A failed callback prevents later
effects and successful-reset recording; preserve CPU state.

```cpu
family operandSystemReset (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0000" named "RESET"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  reset devices
}
```

Complete without effects after opcode fetching.

```cpu
family operandSystemNop (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0001" named "NOP"
}
```

Check privilege before fetching status. Restore SR completely before entering
the halted state.

```cpu
family operandSystemStop (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0010" named "STOP"
  supervisor = flag S
  reject "privilege-violation" if not(supervisor)
  status = fetch word
  perform writeSR(status)
  HALTED <- 1
}
```

Read V and request overflow-trap delivery only when set, preserving every
register and flag.

```cpu
family operandSystemTrapv (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0111 0110" named "TRAPV"
  overflow = flag V
  reject "overflow-trap" if overflow
}
```

TRAP, ILLEGAL, and the two software emulator lines return their named reason
without changing state in the body. The core owns vector selection, saved-PC
rules, and delivery. All sixteen TRAP literals and every low-bit pattern on
lines A/F share those rules; these literals do not add documented instruction
forms to the coverage denominator.

Request illegal-instruction delivery; vector selection and frame entry belong
to the CPU boundary.

```cpu
family operandSystemIllegal (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1010 1111 1100" named "ILLEGAL"
  reject "illegal-instruction"
}
```

Request trap delivery; the CPU boundary uses the opcode literal to select the
vector and owns frame entry.

```cpu
family operandSystemTrap (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "0100 1110 0100 xxxx" named "TRAP #n"
  reject "trap"
}
```

Request line-A software-emulator delivery without instruction-body state
changes.

```cpu
family operandSystemLineA (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "1010 xxxx xxxx xxxx" named "LINE_A"
  reject "line-a"
}
```

Request line-F software-emulator delivery without instruction-body state
changes.

```cpu
family operandSystemLineF (mode: 3, code: 3) {
  encoding "1111 xxxx xxxx xxxx" named "LINE_F"
  reject "line-f"
}
```

## Checks and remaining boundaries

The [register tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-registers.test.ts)
independently enumerate operation words and check widths, aliases, both stack
banks, live upper bits, and failure after every register/flag access. They also
cover every MOVEQ immediate byte and incoming flag combination. The
[chapter tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000.test.ts)
check schema and encoding ownership, memory order, alignment faults,
physical projection, status packing/restoration, and formal edits that change
execution. The [MOVE tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-moves.test.ts)
independently enumerate all 9,150 non-register MOVE forms and compare every
access, state change, and injected failure. The [logical tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-logic.test.ts)
cover all 6,660 logical/unary encodings, independent bit truth tables, callback
mutations, and partial failures. [Logical chapter tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000-logic.test.ts)
verify encoding ownership, shared aliases, and edits to calculations and fields.
The [arithmetic tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-arithmetic.test.ts)
independently scan all 13,510 arithmetic operation words and verify signed and
unsigned results, X and cumulative Z, active stack banks, live callback changes,
and every partial-failure boundary. [Arithmetic chapter tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000-arithmetic.test.ts)
change calculations, flag policies, quick amounts, and paired-register selection
to prove those rules control generated execution. The
[bit/shift tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-bits.test.ts)
independently scan all 5,284 operation words, compare against a bit-array oracle
for every six-bit count and sign boundary, and inject failures after each effect.
[Bit/shift chapter tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000-bits.test.ts)
change loop initials, overflow accumulation, count masks, and bit-number reduction;
[typed-loop tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-iteration-values.test.ts)
check simultaneous updates and their scopes, effects, and outcomes.
The [word/decimal tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-word-and-decimal.test.ts)
independently enumerate all 2,426 encodings, compare every effect and injected
failure, check quotient limits with BigInt, and exhaust packed bytes with
separate digit and valid-decimal oracles. [Word/decimal chapter tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000-word-and-decimal.test.ts)
change product signedness, overflow flags, signed bounds, decimal correction,
cumulative zero, and paired selectors to prove the chapter controls execution.
The [control](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-control.test.ts),
[transfer](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-transfers.test.ts), and
[system probes](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-system.test.ts)
independently enumerate all remaining encodings, compare every observable effect
and failed access, and cover displacement/counter limits, every condition,
register masks, status words, both stack banks, and live callback mutations.
[Chapter mutation tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000-control-and-transfers.test.ts)
change conditions, cursor placement, alternate-byte spacing, mask order and
iteration count, sign extension, privilege and commit order, return-frame reads,
and the RESET encoding/signal. [Boundary-language tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-control-boundary.test.ts)
check distinct cursor/target effects, callback failures, widths, capabilities,
and document-located errors.
[Address-decoder tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/68000-addresses.test.ts)
compare every mode with an independent imperative reference, inject failures
after each observable effect, and exhaust all 65,536 brief index extensions in
both stack banks. [Chapter edits](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-68000-addresses.test.ts)
change bank selection, byte increments, extension signedness, PC-relative capture
order, and staging itself. [Shared staging tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/register-updates.test.ts)
check lazy reads, commit order, repeated commits, and partial failures;
[language tests](../../../../tests/components/cpus/semantics/literate-staging.test.ts)
exercise another CPU and reject hidden staging effects.
Source and family inputs are captured numeric values, not additional hardware registers.

The chapter now resolves effective addresses, selects storage, and stages
register updates. A shared instruction-local helper retains pending values and
commits their generated writes at the chapter's explicit commit points. The native
core extracts encoded fields and owns fetching, retirement, reset, and exception entry.
The remaining TypeScript definition adapter groups chapter families by the
native calling conventions; it authors no instruction behavior. These are
migration boundaries, not absent CPU behavior. Cycle timing and later 680x0 extensions remain outside
the declared model.
